# Backlog-depth supervision issue ledger

Updated: 2026-07-19 09:10 America/New_York

This is the supervisor-owned ledger for defects and unresolved findings discovered while bringing the automated backlog pipeline to a working baseline. Generated atoms, dossiers, options, plans, and tickets remain system-owned evidence; this ledger records work that the supervising Codex process must ensure is addressed rather than silently losing it between reruns.

## Operating rules

- Every finding receives a stable `BDS-*` identifier, objective impact, evidence, next action, and closure proof.
- `open` and `in_progress` findings must be considered before advancing the affected case or stage.
- A finding is closed only with recorded evidence. A workaround is marked `mitigated`, not `closed`.
- Case-local authoring defects go back to the same author/session while it is making progress. Restart is reserved for clearly nonprogressing, uncorrectable, or effectively fundamental rework.
- The ledger is not a reason to block useful throughput. Findings are prioritized by whether they prevent real evidence, deep research, a causally adequate plan, implementation, or an honest outcome disposition.

## Required invariants

- Use the signed-in host Codex route and subscription provenance; do not fall back to per-token API billing.
- Use `gpt-5.6-sol` with `model_reasoning_effort=high` for current pipeline work unless the user changes the model again.
- Use narrow stage-local reruns until each stage works, then perform a minimal full end-to-end proof.
- Do not substitute supervisor-authored research, options, plans, or implementation for system-authored work.
- Do not impose short test timeouts merely because a valid suite is slow.

## Active findings

### BDS-044 - Premerge infrastructure failure overwrote an accepted causal review

- Status: `closed_controller_correction_merged_pr216`
- Priority: delivery-blocking, outcome-integrity
- Objective impact: a storage/setup failure before the original-scenario role ran was represented as proof that the implementation did not address the mechanism. This both blocks good throughput and corrupts the causal record with a fabricated critical finding.
- Exact evidence: PR #213 remained open, cleanly mergeable, and green at exact reviewed head `c930a95f6e768ad0674df91a6b61c3c93d55380d`. The retained same-author `report.json` in review run `20260715T212016Z/codex/0` records `approved`, `mechanism_addressed`, `exercised`, and `closed`. `git worktree add` then failed with `No space left on device` before creating a role artifact. The former catch-all path wrote `premerge_original_scenario_failure.json` with `role_artifact_path: null`, replaced the accepted review with `changes_requested`, and added an unsupported finding titled `Original evidence-backed scenario still fails`.
- Root cause: disposable outcome worktrees and retained-oracle lookup were implicitly tied to the controller's `I:` runs tree, and `_cmd_review_merge` classified `OSError`, setup `RuntimeError`, and contract `ValueError` identically to a completed outcome role whose causal predicates failed.
- Required correction: provide explicit absolute host overrides for disposable outcome worktrees and trusted retained-run/oracle assets; classify only `OutcomeRoleDidNotPass` as a causal rejection; preserve accepted review state for setup/transport/contract blocks; and recover legacy catch-all damage from the retained report without another model turn. Recovery must remain bound to the original PR URL and reviewed head, including retained report/review-reference head receipts, so it cannot launder approval onto changed code.
- Follow-up correction and focused proof: moving disposable outcome worktrees to `U:` exposed Git's cross-volume ownership check. Worktree setup first failed with `detected dubious ownership`; after that caller was corrected, runner_core's internal `rev-parse`, `merge-base`, and `status` calls hit the same boundary. Both layers now grant trust only to the exact resolved repository/workspace on each invocation via `git -c safe.directory=<exact-path> ...`; no global Git configuration is modified. The focused original-scenario premerge replay then passed at exact head `c930a95f6e768ad0674df91a6b61c3c93d55380d`: exit 0, both predicates passed, and `execution_integrity` was true. Artifact: `I:\code\usertest_backlog_depth\runs\usertest_implement\_premerge_outcome_roles\03f0d43eb78e28fa\20260715T235525805412Z\original_scenario\outcome_role.json`.
- Merge result: PR #213 merged at `2026-07-16T01:01:41Z` as `2221377fc5ac6e56b99222e81b0485bad2887a03`. The system preserved the accepted review, reran the unchanged original scenario successfully, moved the ticket to `5 - complete`, and honestly retained `unverified` rather than fabricating mitigation after the separate live-role contract defect in BDS-048.
- Closure proof: focused tests cover path overrides, exact command-local Git trust, causal-role failure versus infrastructure block, legacy recovery, mismatch refusal, and idempotency. The exact PR #213 premerge role passed against `c930a95`, and the merge lifecycle preserved the accepted causal review at `2221377`. The systemic controller correction was included in PR #216, passed exact-head CI and system review, and merged to `dev` as `e0527e23`.

### BDS-045 - Historical semantic scratch retention amplified I: usage by over 125 GiB

- Status: `operationally_recovered_retention_control_missing`
- Priority: operational, throughput-degrading
- Objective impact: canonical pipeline evidence was small, but historical `semantic_runtime` trees retained copied environments, caches, and seeded Codex/plugin homes as though they were durable evidence. On I:'s 256-KiB exFAT clusters this consumed enough allocation to block ordinary work even though logical content was much smaller.
- Exact evidence: 20 terminal agent-todoist and 34 terminal agent-orchestrator `semantic_runtime` trees contained 345,123 files, 186,605 subdirectories, and 33,230,231,314 logical bytes. Their conservative I: allocation floor was 169,876,127,744 bytes versus 34,845,724,672 bytes after exact mirrored preservation on U: NTFS. All 54 sources were moved and digest-verified with no copy, permission, path, or removal error. Two orchestrator records still marked `running` were deliberately retained. Host subscription data at `C:\Users\jason\.codex` was not touched.
- Related evidence: five cold usertest cache/temp roots, 35 Open Meal semantic runtimes, `echo_sandbox`, `utmp`, `claude_sandbox`, an old repair backup, a host PR environment, and video output were also preserved under `U:\I-drive-cold-storage`. The durable migration manifest is `U:\I-drive-cold-storage\MIGRATION_MANIFEST_2026-07-15.md`. I: ended with 370.618 GiB free but still reports `Warning / Full Repair Needed`.
- Required correction: make terminal semantic scratch explicitly disposable or relocatable after canonical database/evidence persistence, retain credential-sensitive copies only outside synchronized roots, and distinguish stale nonterminal database state from active execution before cleanup. Do not remove or move host signed-in Codex data. Do not treat this one-time archival recovery as a retention policy.
- Closure proof: subsequent terminal runs keep canonical evidence while semantic scratch is automatically bounded or moved; stale `running` records receive an explicit terminal/state disposition; two consecutive supervised cycles do not recreate material I: allocation growth.

### BDS-048 - Supervisor guidance overruled a correct immutable-outcome-contract rejection

- Status: `closed_case_verified_and_systemic_gate_merged_pr216`
- Priority: outcome-integrity, delivery-quality
- Objective impact: PR #213 could merge production and ordinary regression work while its mandatory live and mitigation commands were impossible to execute. This allowed code/test throughput but prevented the system from proving that the selected solution actually produced the planned real-world outcome.
- Exact merged defect: the completed plan requires `packages/runner_core/tests/test_maintenance_image_cleanup_isolated_daemon.py::test_protected_external_container_and_partial_error_controls` and `...::test_batch_and_direct_capacity_recovery_pairs`. Merge commit `2221377fc5ac6e56b99222e81b0485bad2887a03` instead contained three differently named tests under `apps/usertest_implement/tests`. Neither planned node had ever existed, and none of the fifteen required stdout evidence markers existed in the merged Python tree. The postmerge live role therefore exited before collection with `file or directory not found`, and outcome progression correctly remained `unverified`.
- Supervisor-caused acceptance: the first automated review in `20260715T211224Z/codex/0/review_summary.json` correctly classified the untouched planned path and absent live result as an error. The focused supervisor prompt in `20260715T212016Z/codex/0/prompt.txt` then incorrectly said the stale planned path was non-authoritative, relocation was advisory-only, and missing literal outcome strings were not a defect. The corrected review followed that instruction and approved. Flexible implementation placement and immutable verification executability were wrongly conflated; this was not nondeterministic model noise.
- Additional real mechanics defects: the merged opt-in module parsed `docker port` before publication could be observed and used GNU-only `df -B1 --output=avail` against a BusyBox DIND image. Ordinary CI skipped the module without all five isolated-Docker settings, and implementation verification never ran it.
- Current correction: the exact original implementation author/session `019f6766-c0f5-7253-b2f0-00b03d1c046a` corrected the postmerge branch using Desktop-bundled Codex `0.144.2`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, and the signed-in host ChatGPT subscription. PR #215 merged the exact planned nodes, port-publication polling, BusyBox-compatible fail-fast free-space probe, and real digest-pinned isolated-DIND controls. The unchanged original scenario and the exact live and mitigation roles then passed at verification commit `49d88252`; the case advanced to `mitigated`, not `resolved`.
- Required systemic correction: at the clean exact reviewed head, preflight mandatory original, live, and mitigation commands before merge. For recognized `python -m pytest path::node` commands, require the path and node to exist and retain a side-effect-free collection receipt. A definite missing path or node is a correction-required outcome-contract defect. Unknown command shapes remain reviewable rather than generically rejected. Ordinary alternate test placement remains a scope advisory; only immutable outcome-command executability is hardened.
- Closure proof: both exact planned nodes collect and pass with all five explicit DIND settings and no skip/xfail; the live node emits its three exact markers from asserted observations; the mitigation node emits all twelve true markers from real paired observations; the unchanged original scenario passes; all roles rerun against the correction commit; outcome records `mitigated`, not `resolved`; and a controller regression proves alternate implementation placement may be advisory while a definitely missing mandatory outcome path/node prevents merge.
- Merge proof: the systemic preflight/outcome-contract gate was included in PR #216, passed exact-head CI and system review, and merged to `dev` as `e0527e23`.

IDEA-originated work remains excluded.

### BDS-052 - Outcome lifecycle writes amplified Windows line endings and caused false plan drift

- Status: `closed_real_reconciliation_and_controller_merged_pr216`
- Priority: current-case blocking, provenance integrity
- Objective impact: the verification-amendment binder correctly requires the completed ticket to remain bound to its immutable exported body and plan, but repeated lifecycle writes changed malformed Windows newline bytes without changing the plan text. The real bind therefore stopped before mutation with `Durable outcome ticket provenance is stale or cross-plan: ticket_body_sha256`.
- Exact evidence: the completed ticket contains 15,355 repeated carriage-return-before-line-feed sequences, currently mostly `0D 0D 0D 0A`. Existing canonical normalization interprets the extra carriage returns as extra blank lines, yielding current body hash `983aa59f...` instead of stored/export hash `05352358...`. Normalizing one-or-more carriage returns immediately before line feed as one newline reproduces the immutable stored body hash `05352358a76a10fb6395ae252b2b476c67fea02f797992ad801cff4713d22f0f` and plan hash `349351ebc6d4afe69369575eee77c7fbc1a93e6727078c04a36bfda0fb90be1f` exactly. Actual content was not changed.
- Root cause: atomic outcome ticket writers decode existing bytes and then use platform text newline translation, adding another carriage return before existing line feeds on Windows. Canonical normalization handled ordinary CRLF and lone CR but treated repeated CR-before-LF as multiple logical newlines.
- Required correction: canonicalize repeated CR-before-LF as one newline, preserve ordinary CRLF/lone-CR semantics, and make atomic ticket writes byte-stable across repeated Windows lifecycle updates. Retain immutable body/plan hash rejection for actual content or cross-plan drift.
- Current proof: focused fixtures preserve the immutable export hashes across repeated Windows lifecycle writes and still reject substantive drift. The real amendment bind succeeded without weakening the stored body or plan hashes. The terminal verifier reopened the historical file with all 15,355 repeated CR-before-LF sequences, recovered the Stage-6 contract, and returned zero errors.
- Closure proof: a completed-ticket fixture with repeated lifecycle writes still matches its export-time hashes; identical amendment binding is byte-preserving; actual plan text drift remains rejected; the real PR #215 amendment binds atomically without weakening stored provenance.
- Merge proof: the newline-safe lifecycle correction was included in PR #216 and merged to `dev` as `e0527e23`.

### BDS-055 - Secondary postprocessing overwrote a precise missing-artifact failure

- Status: `closed_broad_qualification_and_pr216_merge_complete`
- Priority: validation integrity, self-healing feedback quality
- Objective impact: a selected implementation attempt could pass its broker verification but lose a required retained artifact. The runner initially detected that exact evidence-integrity failure, then replaced it with `binary_or_command_missing`. Incorrect causal feedback can send the same author or supervisor toward Codex installation/authentication instead of repairing the actual missing artifact.
- Exact evidence: the broad delivery/runtime partition passed 927 checks with four skips and failed this one node. The isolated node reproduced in 6.19 seconds. Its artifacts show agent exit 0, broker source `broker_reuse`, successful verification, matched workspace state, and no missing broker response artifacts. The fake agent deliberately removed only `raw_events.attempt1.jsonl`. `error_traceback.txt` shows all-attempt normalization reopened that missing path after final materialization had already written `selected_attempt_artifacts_incomplete`; the outer catch then overwrote `error.json`, and the generic text classifier mislabeled `No such file or directory` as a missing binary.
- Root cause: all-attempt event normalization introduced after the selected-artifact fail-closed contract used the attempt path even after materialization had recorded it missing and created a canonical placeholder. The outer exception path treated any later postprocessing exception as the primary terminal cause.
- Local correction: after a known selected-artifact materialization failure, normalize the canonical placeholder rather than reopening absent attempt paths. If later postprocessing still raises, retain the existing specific `error.json` and write the secondary exception to `postprocess_error.json`. Host `CODEX_HOME`, signed-in `.codex` data, host-login enforcement, API-key clearing, and subscription provenance are unchanged.
- Current proof: the corrected missing-artifact path and an injected secondary-normalization failure both retain `selected_attempt_artifacts_incomplete`; the latter writes the secondary artifact without changing the primary cause. Both parametrized cases plus healthy broker-reuse and healthy multi-attempt aggregation controls pass 4/4 in 55.7 seconds.
- Closure proof: on frozen commit `f46d751b`, the complete delivery/runtime partition passed 929 tests with four skips and zero failures in 1,157.4 seconds. PR #216 preserved both specific failure classification and successful multi-attempt normalization, passed exact-head CI, and merged to `dev` as `e0527e23`.

### BDS-056 - Qualification supervision lost sibling results and mutated sealed inputs

- Status: `operational_correction_verified_current_cycle`
- Priority: supervision efficiency, validation integrity
- Objective impact: useful broad-suite work was discarded or invalidated even though the underlying system was making progress. This increases intervention cost and can create false product failures, directly opposing the throughput objective.
- Exact evidence: a direct `Promise.all` rejected when the backlog partition failed, discarded the already-exited CLI summary, and severed the still-running delivery process from its result channel. The delivery process finished after about 19 minutes but produced no usable receipt, requiring one standalone replay. During the backlog replay, concurrent dashboard/ledger edits changed the sealed `git status --porcelain` hash; the exact transaction test passed on stable bytes.
- Required operational correction: use independent result cells or all-settled capture with a durable per-partition result artifact; never rely on one combined fail-fast payload. Freeze and preferably commit the exact tree before git-status-sensitive qualification, and make no repository writes until each sealed partition exits. Treat lost or supervisor-invalidated results as unknown, never as pass or product failure.
- Current proof: the lost delivery result was not counted. The final backlog, delivery/runtime, and CLI/tools partitions ran in independent cells on frozen `f46d751b` and retained exact results: 1,735/1, 929/4, and 141/3 passed/skipped respectively, all with zero failures. No repository write occurred while they ran, and no result process was orphaned. The two exact isolated-Docker tests then passed in 954.4 seconds with zero provisioner DIND containers before and after.
- Closure proof: the current cycle demonstrates the corrected operating method. The future automated qualification runner should encode independent durable result capture and a sealed-tree lease rather than depend on supervisor discipline; until then this remains an operationally verified manual control, not autonomous closure.

### BDS-057 - Local qualification omitted isolated changed-project boundaries before PR publication

- Status: `closed_exact_head_ci_and_guarded_merge_complete`
- Priority: delivery efficiency, validation integrity
- Objective impact: frozen component qualification was correctly green for the causal pipeline behavior, but it did not reproduce every changed project's isolated CI command. Three branch-owned defects therefore reached the first PR matrix, adding a correction cycle after publication and delaying the first shadow run without improving research or solution quality.
- Exact evidence: draft PR #216 at `e94b6ce8e217fa75aa04295ffa7c0488e78b0bc0` triggered push run `29478853671` and pull-request run `29478913132`. Each completed 40 jobs with 37 passes and the same three failures; the duplicate triggers are three unique failures, not six. `backlog_miner` failed Ruff `I001` on one import block in `test_full_chain_causal_acceptance.py`. `runner_core` failed only `test_failed_legacy_sandbox_expectation_can_be_revalidated_without_mutation` because the Windows-only failed-receipt premise is intentionally false on POSIX project-execpolicy. `usertest_backlog` failed only `test_materialized_repair_is_independently_readjudicated_and_records_final_cycle` because branch commit `ce248229` replaced valid sibling-test imports with a monorepo-root `apps.*` path that isolated package execution does not expose.
- Root cause: final local qualification used broad repository-source partitions and selected-file Ruff rather than the exact changed-project scaffold commands. That masked the app test's accidental repository-root import, did not exercise the Windows-specific fixture under Linux semantics, and omitted the newly changed backlog_miner file from lint. This is one CI-equivalence coverage gap with three concrete manifestations, not three new pipeline-control categories.
- Correction: Ruff organized the backlog_miner import without semantic change; the legacy sandbox-revalidation test is now explicitly skipped outside native Windows rather than weakening POSIX or Windows production verification; and the app transaction test restores its prior package-local sibling imports without adding `sys.path`, repository-root `PYTHONPATH`, or a cross-project `tests` package. Signed-in host Codex data, host-login enforcement, API-key clearing, subscription provenance, and production execution policy are unchanged.
- Current proof: package-wide Ruff passes for `backlog_miner`, `runner_core`, and `usertest_backlog`; the exact Windows runner test passes; the exact CI-path `usertest_backlog` project suite passes in 334.2 seconds; and the previously run full-chain acceptance test passes after the import ordering correction. A combined long-running local verification channel was interrupted and its result was not counted; the authoritative owning-boundary reruns are retained instead.
- Closure update: replacement CI on `9190f49e` passed both 40-job triggers. The final documentation/provenance correction head `f6c93c97` also reached 80/80 observed green checks after one push-trigger Windows job was rerun. Its 27-test canonical smoke had passed and only GitHub-hosted post-job cleanup hooks failed without an exception; the identical PR job and targeted rerun passed, so no product or workflow change was made for that transient event. PR #216 then merged through a head-SHA guard as `e0527e23`.
- Replacement-CI proof: corrected head `9190f49e6f483ace34f706c1e43645d5bdd5b397` passed push run `29480914169` and pull-request run `29480916329`, each 40/40. These are the same 40 unique checks under duplicate triggers, not 80 independent checks. The immediate correction is verified; full closure still requires the next PR candidate to execute its exact changed-project boundaries before publication rather than learning the same class from CI.
- Required operational action: before first push, derive every changed project and run its configured package lint/test boundary, while retaining narrower tests for fast iteration and broader causal partitions for cross-project confidence. Capture each long result independently so one tool interruption cannot discard completed siblings. Do not restart the full backlog pipeline for a package-local validation defect.
- Closure proof: replacement push and pull-request CI at the corrected head pass all project and smoke jobs. The next PR candidate runs exact changed-project boundaries before publication and does not repeat this class of post-publication correction.

### BDS-004 — Progressing research is prematurely restarted by a cost heuristic

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: objective-blocking
- Objective impact: destroys good throughput and prevents self-healing even when the same researcher has materially improved its result.
- Evidence: `stage3_maintenance_supervised_boundary_20260714T112500Z` ended as `restart:correction_cost_reached_investigation_cost`. Attempt 30 moved to `research_status=evidence_sufficient`, `actionability_assessment.disposition=requires_change`, zero blockers, and zero material unknowns, but had correctable verifier findings.
- Local correction: persisted `evidence_verification_feedback` is now restored as an external-review frontier; resolved prior findings are evaluated before the cost cutoff; and a safe transformed-error candidate becomes the forward correction baseline without displacing the objective best. A candidate that retains old findings and adds more still rolls back.
- Verification: nine focused tests and the full `test_research_runner.py` suite pass (101 tests in 65.94 seconds).
- Recurrence evidence: `stage3_maintenance_attempt36_docker_recovered_20260714T194200Z` again stopped as `restart:correction_cost_reached_investigation_cost` after attempt 45 crossed 603.289 correction seconds against a 468.664-second original investigation. Attempt 45 had nevertheless reworked four missing atom bindings into four concrete bindings that the verifier could reject more precisely as `not_bound_to_observation`. The runner compared attempt 45 only with the six-error objective-best frontier and ignored attempt 44's immediate 12-error feedback frontier, so it recorded no resolved errors and let cost alone stop the same author. Attempts 43-45 and their workspace changes remain retained; none is accepted for optioning.
- Required systemic correction: measure immediate-feedback progress separately from objective-best progress. A safe equal-count replacement continues without resetting the cost clock; a lower immediate-feedback count may reset it. Cost is a secondary signal and may stop only repeated observed nonprogress or fundamental rework, never the first changed/no-op correction by itself. Keep the six-error objective best unchanged until a genuinely better dossier is verified.
- Current local correction: the adaptive repair loop now retains an immediate-feedback frontier separately from the safe forward and objective-best frontiers. Equal-count identity rework continues without resetting cost, a smaller immediate-feedback set resets the next-turn cost clock without automatic promotion, and a cost restart requires at least two consecutive genuine-nonprogress turns. Consecutive changing transport failures still consume the same bounded economics.
- Current verification: five focused policy/integration regressions pass, and the complete `test_research_runner.py` module passed 107/107 in 62.8 seconds before the later churn-safeguard additions; the current superset passes 111/111 in 86.7 seconds.
- Live verification result: the same Terra author did continue instead of being immediately restarted and produced 11 retained correction runs under `stage3_maintenance_same_author_oracle_repair_20260714T204137Z`. That proves the immediate-feedback/cost fix restored author continuity, but it exposed the separate substantive-frontier defect in BDS-018: changing or smaller verifier sets could reset progress indefinitely while stronger causal content disappeared. The orphaned outer process was stopped at 17:54 after the loop met the explicit nonprogress threshold. Its exact Python/Docker-exec descendants are gone, Docker has zero containers, and no artifact, image, or workspace was removed.
- Closure proof: met. Focused policy tests pass and the same maintenance researcher received its verifier findings and produced 11 retained next attempts instead of a premature cost restart. The newly exposed substantive-churn behavior is tracked separately as BDS-018 rather than keeping the corrected cost-policy defect open.

### BDS-018 - Mechanical verifier churn can erase substantive research and run indefinitely

- Status: `reclassified_under_BDS-019`
- Priority: objective-blocking, throughput-degrading
- Objective impact: a correction loop can look mechanically active while moving away from the actual objective. It may reduce or replace validation findings by deleting an independently supported root-cause facet or executable success contract, and changing error identities can then keep the same loop alive without producing a decision-ready dossier.
- Evidence: the first two retained runs in `stage3_maintenance_same_author_oracle_repair_20260714T204137Z` preserved two root-cause hypotheses, including cleanup-before-build recovery, and an explicit positive outcome contract. Later candidates reduced the mechanical frontier to one or two findings but retained only the age/count hypothesis and dropped the operational positive contract. Runs from `20260714T210300Z` through `20260714T214308Z` cycled among `research_falsification_intervention_invalid`, `primary_falsification_survival_missing`, a seven-error causal cascade, and a two-error falsification pair. Eleven complete candidates were retained; none restored the stronger causal coverage. A twelfth replay had begun when supervision paused it.
- Required correction: retain three distinct notions: immediate-feedback progress, mechanical objective best, and substantive coverage. Fewer verifier findings count as improvement only when established, independently evidenced causal coverage and executable outcome coverage are not silently removed. Unsupported substantive loss should quarantine the candidate and return precise restore feedback to the same author. After a small consecutive sequence with no mechanical-objective or substantive advancement, return a resumable `repairable_paused` disposition with the strongest frontier and all candidates retained; do not restart or discard the investigation.
- Guardrail against over-hardening: this is not a requirement for perfect dossiers or fixed benchmark-specific fields. Evidence-backed epistemic downgrades remain valid, one or two imperfect corrections remain repairable, and any non-regressing lower-error candidate resets progress normally. The pause exists to hand control back for better feedback, not to block useful throughput.
- Local correction: the runner now projects general substantive proof roles (supported/mechanism/falsification hypothesis coverage, origin-atom experimental coverage, controlled production touchpoints, and executable outcome coverage) independently of raw error count. Unsupported loss is quarantined against the safe frontier and receives exact restoration feedback; three consecutive losses pause with retained work. Equal-count error-identity churn remains forward work for two corrections and pauses on the third. A lower error count, deeper verifier frontier, restored/added substantive coverage, or evidence-backed epistemic revision resets the applicable churn state.
- Verification: the complete `test_research_runner.py` module now passes 123/123 in 126.7 seconds with a one-hour ceiling and external temp storage; the six resume-helper tests, the three controlled-call identity checks, Ruff, `py_compile`, and scoped `git diff --check` also pass. The suite proves unsupported-downgrade, substantive-loss, lateral-churn, and ordinary-nonadvancing state is retained rather than restarted or discarded.
- Corrected accounting: the numbered supervision ledger through record 48 is not a count of model turns. It contains 40 completed model reports, seven verifier-only feedback snapshots (records 3, 7, 11, 14, 20, 23, and 29), and one model-free recovery record (46). A filesystem audit found 62 completed `gpt-5.6-terra` reports for this same maintenance case, totaling 10,885.2 seconds of recorded agent wall time, plus the interrupted run labeled attempt 49. The old 46/48-attempt narrative understated the actual retry volume and mixed unlike record types.
- Controller-failure evidence: numbered attempt 19 was verifier-clean and causally sufficient. Forty-seven of the 62 completed model reports, consuming 8,219.07 seconds (about 76% of the total), occurred after that point. The earliest defensible controller stop was numbered attempt 11; even under a conservative standard the absolute latest defensible stop was attempt 20. Continuing through attempt 48 and starting attempt 49 was therefore not justified research iteration. It was a controller and stage-contract failure, tracked as BDS-019.
- Current correction: the counter now recovers only from hash-valid, same-session retained sibling attempts bound to the exact safe source and error frontier, carries through independent-feedback transitions, and pauses after the general three-turn threshold without restarting or deleting work. Authenticated prior feedback and the latest supervisor note are now foregrounded before the full contract; an unsupported downgrade requires newly executed, retained, dossier-cited counterevidence. This is a correction-economics control, not a universal tool-count or quality gate.
- Current disposition: no additional same-author Stage 3 retry is warranted. The run labeled attempt 49 was interrupted, its partial artifacts were retained, and its exact orphan processes were stopped. No artifact, Docker image, container, cache, workspace, or run directory was deleted or moved. A real Stage 4 handoff already produced one count-only option, which Stage 5 rejected before planning. The next proof is immutable persisted-evidence authentication followed by same-session Stage 4 revision using the exact retained Stage 5 feedback.
- Closure proof: focused and full runner tests still cover candidate quarantine and repair-state persistence, but BDS-018 is no longer the governing explanation for this case. BDS-019 must prove that a causally sufficient dossier exits Stage 3 instead of entering further corrective turns.

### BDS-019 - Controller continued Stage 3 after causal sufficiency

- Status: `closed_for_current_case_monitoring_generalization`
- Priority: objective-blocking, throughput-destroying
- Objective impact: the pipeline spent most of this case's model time trying to satisfy misplaced future-solution proof requirements after the root mechanism was already established. One real Stage 4 handoff produced a shallow option that Stage 5 correctly rejected, but the controller then routed those solution-design gaps back into Stage 3. Accepted-option and plan throughput remained zero, and a working researcher looked like repeated research failure.
- Record-type audit: the legacy sequence numbered 1-48 comprises 40 model reports, seven verifier-only snapshots, and one model-free recovery record. Those numbers must never again be reported as 48 model attempts.
- Filesystem audit: 62 completed same-case `gpt-5.6-terra` reports total 10,885.2 seconds of recorded wall time, plus an interrupted 63rd model run labeled attempt 49. Of the completed reports, 40 are directly numbered, 11 belong to an orphan continuation chain, and 11 are unnumbered side/adjudicated runs.
- Sufficiency point: numbered attempt 19 is verifier-clean, records `research_status=evidence_sufficient`, has zero verification errors, binds all three source atoms across six experiments, and establishes the production count-or-age mechanism with the controlled 49-fresh versus 3-aged result. It is the canonical causal Stage 3 artifact.
- Stop-policy assessment: attempt 11 was the earliest defensible stop because the causal direction and production locus were already established. Attempt 19 was fully verifier-clean. Attempt 20 is the absolute latest defensible stop under any reasonable allowance for confirmation. The 47 completed model reports after attempt 19 consumed 8,219.07 seconds, approximately 76% of the total measured model time, without creating a legitimate reason to remain in Stage 3.
- Root cause: Stage 3 readiness required a verified post-change operational outcome contract whose exact solution behavior properly belongs to optioning, falsification, planning, and outcome verification. That circular boundary made research prove a future implementation before Stage 4 was allowed to design it. The controller then treated downstream design work as missing research and kept issuing correction turns.
- Required correction: Stage 3 must end when current evidence establishes a real unactioned case, a source-bound causal mechanism, the production locus, counterevidence or deterministic causal closure, actionability, relation disposition, and no root-material unknown. Stage 4 must define candidate success properties and safety constraints; Stage 5 must adversarially review their causal adequacy; Stage 6 must bind them to exact executable before/after checks; the outcome stage must supply actual post-change proof. Preserve stronger legacy research outcome evidence when present, but do not require future-solution proof to leave Stage 3.
- Local correction: the Stage 3 qualification-repair adapter now advances from the controller's current authenticated payload instead of reloading the older dossier collection on every correction. An authenticated ready payload with valid persisted evidence short-circuits additional research; cost remains telemetry, and the general three-turn material-nonprogress rule retains the strongest frontier rather than restarting it.
- Verification: all 23 qualification-repair runtime/materialization tests plus three focused current-payload regressions pass. The retained attempt-19 dossier advanced through one Stage 4 option, one Stage 5 selection, and one Stage 6 plan without another Stage 3 model invocation. The final assembled chain remains post-change unverified; this proof closes the current case's stage-exit defect, not its implementation outcome or generalization to unseen cases.
- Closure proof: met for the current case. Attempt 19 passes corrected Stage 3 readiness and persisted-evidence authentication without mutation; downstream design findings stayed with Stages 4-6; and the exact chain reached `ready_for_ticket`. Preserve the 62-report, 10,885.2-second overrun as historical controller-failure evidence and monitor the stop behavior on future nondeterministic cases.

### BDS-020 - Plain Windows Codex resolution selected an obsolete npm CLI after restart

- Status: `reopened_stale_installed_runtime_bypassed_resolver`
- Priority: baseline-execution blocking
- Objective impact: every otherwise valid local backlog stage could fail before producing work because `configs/agents.yaml` intentionally configures the portable name `codex`, but Windows `PATH` selected an older npm installation that did not support the requested `gpt-5.6-terra` model. Repeating or repairing model output could not correct this host-launch mismatch.
- Exact evidence: after the 2026-07-14 host restart, `where.exe codex` selected `C:\Users\jason\AppData\Roaming\npm\codex` first and that command reported `codex-cli 0.139.0`. The current Codex Desktop-managed host executable under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenAI\Codex\bin\<opaque-install-id>\codex.exe` reported `codex-cli 0.144.2`. No installation hash or user profile path is encoded in the correction.
- Root cause: local execution, login, command-availability preflight, and CLI-version preflight did not share a Desktop-aware resolver. Ordinary `shutil.which("codex")` therefore allowed the stale npm shim to outrank the current Desktop-managed CLI after environment reconstruction on restart.
- Local correction: one exported resolver now preserves every explicit configured path; for only the plain Windows names `codex` and `codex.exe`, it discovers structurally valid Desktop-managed candidates below `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, orders them by recency, requires a successful local `--version` probe, and otherwise falls back to ordinary `PATH`. The adapter's login and execution paths and the runner's local availability/version preflights use that same decision. Docker-prefixed commands are unchanged and continue to resolve inside the container.
- Subscription boundary: the correction changes only the executable. It retains the canonical host `CODEX_HOME`, exact `Logged in using ChatGPT` requirement, canonical ChatGPT routing overrides, and child-only blanking of `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `CODEX_API_KEY`, `CODEX_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_API_BASE`, `OPENAI_ORG_ID`, and `OPENAI_ORGANIZATION`. It neither copies nor removes host Codex data and introduces no API-key fallback.
- Verification: focused resolver, execution, preflight, and subscription-auth tests pass. The complete affected adapter/backlog-auth/provenance/preflight/exec-policy suite passes with the 24-hour test ceiling. A live non-model probe resolves plain `codex` to the current Desktop-managed executable, records `codex-cli 0.144.2` in the runner version receipt, and reports exact ChatGPT login with all blocked API/provider environment variables blank. No model, Docker operation, cleanup, deletion, or move was performed for this repair.
- Closure proof: met for the baseline blocker. A subsequent real stage invocation still owns model-level throughput proof, but it no longer needs a pinned installation hash or supervisor-selected executable path to reach the supported host Codex CLI.
- Recurrence on controller review: standalone system-review runs `20260716T075924Z/codex/216` and `20260716T080245Z/codex/216` stopped before reviewer dispatch. Their ordinary PowerShell backend probes passed with `shell_probe=ok`, but the installed `apps/usertest/.venv` copy of `agent_adapters` predated the Desktop-aware resolver and selected npm Codex `0.139.0`. Terra returned HTTP 400 requiring a newer CLI; the runner then incorrectly collapsed that model/CLI error into `shell_probe_failed` and advised the already-selected `inspect` policy. Allowlisting `LOCALAPPDATA` did not change the local agent environment. A process-local `PATH` prefix selected the Desktop-managed `0.144.2` binary without installing or editing host software; a live Terra probe and the 380.6-second review then completed through exact ChatGPT login with API/provider variables blank.
- Required follow-up: runtime/package freshness must be part of the entrypoint contract, and agent-level provider/model incompatibility must retain its actual diagnostic rather than masquerading as shell-policy failure. Do not block the current causally reviewed PR merely because the bootstrap controller used a stale venv; prove the next workspace-current invocation selects the Desktop resolver without supervisor `PATH` repair.

### BDS-059 - Atom extraction omitted the causal maintenance-image evidence

- Status: `local_correction_verified_focused_stage1_replay_required`
- Priority: evidence-mining, root-cause depth
- Objective impact: the current atom corpus can surface a generic disk-exhaustion symptom while omitting the exact maintenance-image accumulation, cleanup, and operator-recovery evidence that established the actionable historical mechanism. Research started from that reduced evidence could produce only a surface-level ENOSPC case or miss the unactioned mechanism entirely.
- Exact evidence: the frozen qualification bundle contains 2,781 eligible atoms. The two independent currentness audits reconciled them to zero actionable, 2,771 non-actionable, and ten ambiguous atoms; Auditor B found zero actionable groups. Inspection of the current Stage 1 inputs found only coarse aggregated ENOSPC evidence, not the causal maintenance-image sidecar and operator-recovery evidence that previously drove that case from atoms through implementation PR. This is an extraction/lineage omission, not evidence that the mechanism disappeared.
- Root cause: the runner-owned `sandbox/maintenance_image_cleanup.json` sidecar was not part of the report-history contract, so atom extraction could not observe it. Separately retained research records need an explicit bounded evidence root; derived evidence whose declared parent is absent from the canonical registry must remain unresolved and non-originating rather than manufacturing a case.
- Local correction and review repair: report history reads the exact registered maintenance sidecar with parse/read and content provenance. Atom extraction emits one bounded observation containing recorded counts, or one explicit artifact-error observation without fabricated counts. An independent probe caught that the first implementation inherited the enclosing implementation/verification mission lineage and remained ineligible; source-specific `runner_maintenance_image_cleanup` observation lineage now makes the sidecar mineable without changing ordinary report/research/implementation lineage. Legacy sidecars expose exact tag counts and explicitly named unique-tag-suffix proxies while keeping physical image identity count unknown; optional `kept_image_ids` is reported as observed but not assumed complete. The full 478-test backlog-core suite and Ruff pass. Real July 4, July 7, and July 8 records are eligible with 2/1, 98/49, and 9/5 tag/suffix observations respectively. Bounded retained-root ingestion separately passes its missing-parent fail-closed tests and cannot manufacture this absent case.
- Closure proof: the focused replay produces an atom or explicit disposition bound to the retained causal evidence and recovery observation; the miner distinguishes that mechanism from generic ENOSPC evidence without supervisor-authored problem prose; an unrelated storage-noise control remains non-actionable.

A new direct regression proves an unresolved observation retains no blocker and remains eligible. The two exposed aggregate tests pass, followed by the complete 13-test derived-evidence scope.
- Closure proof: ordinary observation evidence remains mineable; missing-canonical-parent derived evidence remains unresolved, parent-traceable, and ineligible to originate a case.

### BDS-064 - Semantic custody sealed atom-referenced directory existence without its contents

- Status: `closed_real_semantic_recapture_verified`
- Priority: evidence integrity, iteration performance
- Objective impact: atoms can reference a command-failure directory rather than one exact file. Sealing only directory existence would allow evidence used by mining to change after qualification preparation without changing the custody manifest, undermining the claim that plans are based on frozen real evidence.
- Exact evidence: the first real semantic-manifest probe found 814 `non_file` atom references, all command-failure directories such as `command_failures/cmd_01`. The initial implementation retained only an existence marker for them. This was discovered on 98 real history records and 1,966 extracted atoms, not a synthetic-only edge case.
- Correction: only explicitly atom-referenced directories are recursively sealed. Their child directories and files receive deterministic relative entries; nested reparse/symlink escapes are rejected. Unreferenced sandbox trees, workspaces, prompts, bytecode, and verification payloads remain outside the semantic boundary, so the correction does not restore a broad tree scan.
- Closure proof: 33 focused semantic/transaction tests pass with two host-capability symlink-creation skips, 63 reports-backlog integration tests pass, and Ruff passes. Two real captures completed in 33.058 and 32.705 seconds and were byte-identical. The source manifest contains 814 explicit directory markers and zero unsealed non-file references; combined source and implementation custody is 5,559 files / 97,411,870 bytes versus the old 169,241 files / 8,536,816,725-byte full-tree boundary. Recursive traversal added zero unique files and zero bytes because 3,436 child files were already separately atom-referenced. Redundant pre-dedup hashing remains a performance opportunity, not a custody defect, and is deferred until the complete preparation path is measured.

### BDS-065 - Full preparation overwrote trusted maintenance-sidecar observation lineage

- Status: `component_and_real_corpus_verified_live_stage1_replay_pending`
- Priority: zero-throughput, evidence mining, root-cause depth
- Objective impact: the complete model-free preparation contains the exact maintenance-image cleanup observations, but none can reach Stage 1. This recreates the central failure the remediation is meant to solve: real causal evidence exists in the data while mining sees only surface symptoms.
- Exact evidence: the schema-2 bundle at `U:\usertest_backlog_qualification\semantic_prepare_probe_20260716T1134Z\out\76036934e84aaa373ed258e22efee94acaf3272577f2b7cb96947809374d557c\qualification_input_bundle.json` was produced with exit zero in 264.366 monotonic seconds, contains 2,877 atoms, and verifies from disk. It contains 52 `source=maintenance_image_cleanup` atoms and all 52 are excluded by `eligible_problem_mining_atoms`; their final roles are implementation or verification. In the same bundle, all 44 explicitly retained research atoms are `parent_missing`, blocked from mining, and have no case ID, which is the intended non-originating behavior.
- Root cause: `ingest_derived_evidence_records` sends implementation-root atoms through `_bind_derived_atoms`. That binder derives one enclosing-run role and unconditionally overwrites every extracted atom's `origin_stage` and `evidence_role`, including source-specific atoms already authenticated by `lineage_authorities=["runner_operational_sidecar"]`. Focused sidecar probes stopped after extraction and therefore did not cover this later production transformation.
- Correction: `_bind_derived_atoms` now recognizes only extractor-authenticated, observed `runner_*` atoms carrying `lineage_authorities=["runner_operational_sidecar"]` as mission-independent. It preserves their runner stage, observation role, pending lineage, and only adds source-root provenance. Ordinary report atoms still inherit implementation/verification role and exact parent-binding status. Binding metrics explicitly label the runner observations `not_applicable_runner_observation` instead of falsely reporting a missing parent.
- Current proof: two direct ingestion tests cover verified implementation and PR-review parents; both prove the sidecar remains parentless and eligible while same-run report prose remains parent-bound and ineligible. A filesystem-history-to-Stage-1 dry-run assigns both fixture sidecars in `decision_eligible_atom_ids`, excludes both report atoms, and then records the sidecars' legitimate synthesized case disposition. The full affected file passes 16/16 and Ruff passes. The production-scope read-only probe scanned 87/87 matching implementation records (54 Git-remote, 33 local-path), selected 52 sidecar records, ingested 554 atoms, and found 52/52 maintenance observations eligible, zero blockers, and zero `derived_parent_binding_status` fields; 502 report/other atoms remained on the unavailable derived-binding path.
- Closure proof: the component and real-corpus eligibility boundary is met. A small live Stage 1/2 replay with `gpt-5.6-terra`, signed-in host subscription provenance, and export disabled is now permitted. A regenerated full qualification bundle and two complete shadow cycles remain required before BDS-065 and overall qualification close.

### BDS-066 - Subscription preflight passed where the actual Codex execution environment could not activate

- Status: `live_activation_verified_monitoring_required`
- Priority: zero-throughput, subscription provenance
- Objective impact: a component can report exact ChatGPT login and still fail before the model author exists. Treating login status as execution readiness wastes launches; weakening the boundary or copying credentials would violate the required signed-in subscription route.
- Exact evidence: `stage12_maintenance_sidecar_terra_20260716T125500Z` failed in 5.457 seconds because Desktop Codex 0.144.5 emitted two canonical access-denied launcher diagnostics on stderr alongside exact `Logged in using ChatGPT`; the strict parser rejected the invocation before a response. After narrowly normalizing only those two structured diagnostics, `stage12_maintenance_sidecar_terra_20260716T130000Z` passed login on every finalized launch but each execution failed against read-only canonical `C:\Users\jason\.codex\state_5.sqlite`. No API/provider credential was used or copied.
- Correction: the login parser ignores only the exact stale-arg0-cleanup diagnostic and a path-alias diagnostic whose dynamic path is one direct `codex-arg0*` child of canonical `CODEX_HOME\tmp\arg0`; unknown text, stdout diagnostics, API-key status, contradictory status, nonzero exit, and unblanked provider variables remain rejected. The bounded component is launched where canonical host Codex state is writable while preserving the host `CODEX_HOME` and blanking all seven API/provider variables.
- Verification: twelve focused login tests and the combined 31-test subscription/auth scope pass. A live parser probe reports exact ChatGPT status, seven of seven API/provider variables blank, and only the two allowed diagnostic kinds ignored. The final bounded run completed Stage 1/2 in 122.063 seconds with four of four model manifests subscription-verified and no credential copy or API fallback.
- Closure proof: live author activation is now established. Continue checking execution, not only login, in future runtime preflight; recurrence under an allegedly writable environment reopens this issue.

### BDS-067 - Pre-author acquisition failures were unbounded and mislabeled as model corrections

- Status: `local_controller_and_accounting_verified_live_pause_pending`
- Priority: good-throughput efficiency, supervision integrity
- Objective impact: launch failures that never create an author can consume unbounded time and disk while dashboard telemetry falsely credits them as format or same-session model correction. That hides zero throughput and makes intervention trends unreliable.
- Exact evidence: the stopped `stage12_maintenance_sidecar_terra_20260716T130000Z` component retained 191 CLI launches, 190 finalized failed manifests, one interrupted receipt, zero responses, and zero author sessions over at least 455 seconds. Nonce- and path-distinct failure text prevented the old identical-state detector from observing recurrence. The stage metadata projected `len(attempt_history)-1` into both `format_retry_count` and `same_session_correction_count`, even for sessionless acquisition.
- Correction: shared author acquisition now pauses after three consecutive nonadvancing outcomes. Progress requires a lower global normalized-error minimum or a higher global maximum of unique valid item keys; monotonic watermarks prevent oscillation from resetting the boundary. A real session is checked first and wins. All attempts, failures, cost, and the best retained observation survive as `repairable_paused`. Stage 1 metadata now reports `session_acquisition_retry_count` separately, counts completed response-correction turns as format retries, and includes failed resumed invocations only in same-session correction attempts.
- Verification: all 32 prompt-correction tests pass, followed by the 153-test affected correction/Stage 1/Stage 6 scope in 41.6 seconds and Ruff. Four focused Stage 1 telemetry tests prove a fresh session-acquisition retry reports acquisition 1, format 0, and same-session correction 0 while ordinary response repair and resumed invocation failure retain their existing classifications.
- Closure proof: run one bounded live pre-author failure probe and show deterministic pause after the third nonadvancing result without manual process containment. The successful host-write run proves healthy acquisition is not falsely blocked but does not exercise the pause path.

### BDS-068 - Stage 1 depth review rewarded a surface measurement caveat and copied its inferred impact

- Status: `general_prompt_and_reconciliation_fix_verified_same_author_live_correction_pending`
- Priority: root-cause depth, good-throughput quality
- Objective impact: mechanically valid mining can promote an explicit uncertainty marker into the canonical problem, infer impact not present in the atoms, and pass an ostensibly independent depth review without evaluating the stronger observed behavior. That produces detailed-looking but shallow work—the central failure this project is intended to correct.
- Exact evidence: the bounded 122.063-second Stage 1/2 run read three enabled non-dry-run cleanup observations. Chronologically, retained tags / unique retained suffix proxies were 2/1 on July 4, 72/36 on July 6, and 98/49 on July 7; deleted tags, deleted image IDs, and errors stayed zero. Both primary miner and coverage reviewer instead emitted `Image cleanup does not measure physical retained image identities`, asserted operators could not assess storage effect, confidence 0.96, and medium severity. Stage 2 promoted it to P1/research_new. The missing physical count is genuinely an evidence boundary, but these atoms do not show that the measurement gap caused a user failure, physical image growth, byte growth, policy violation, or deletion eligibility.
- General correction: miner and Stage 1 guidance now require comparable structured observations to be ordered chronologically and compared across changed and invariant fields. Measurement caveats bound claims rather than automatically becoming problems. Plausible or unverified impact remains researchable but must not be described as observed. Coverage reconciliation now supports confirm, stable-ID refine, reject, or recover-different-problem instead of requiring verbatim equality to retain support. This is prompt/reconciliation guidance, not a deterministic benchmark-specific hard gate.
- Verification: five exact focused tests, nine related review/reconciliation tests, the complete 98-test Stage 1 evidence/reconciliation suite in 41.4 seconds, 41 prompt-lint tests, Ruff, prompt-lint CLI, and diff checks pass. Generic telemetry-series fixtures—not image-specific constants—cover chronology, caveat boundaries, honest unknown impact, and stable-ID refinement.
- Production disposition boundary: the component used an intentionally empty case registry, so its `keep_separate` and `research_new` outputs cannot establish novelty. The same July 4 and July 7 artifact hashes already support `case:maintenance-image-burst-retention-unbounded`, now mitigated through PRs 213 and 215. A populated-registry replay must bind these as pre-fix support, not post-fix recurrence or a new case.
- Closure proof: the exact coverage reviewer receives the evidence-bound critique, returns a retained/refined/retracted result without restart, and the corrected result survives relation review and Stage 2. A separate populated-registry replay attaches the evidence to the existing mitigated case or exposes a real lineage gap without supervisor-authored suppression.

### BDS-071 - Corrected non-support atoms retained active case membership across registry replay

- Status: `closed_retraction_and_populated_graph_preservation_verified`
- Priority: root-cause quality, self-healing correctness
- Objective impact: the reviewer can correctly retract a shallow case, yet the persistent graph restores it on the next cycle. That converts an explicit correction back into bad throughput and defeats the stable-case objective.
- Exact evidence: `stage12_maintenance_correction_20260716T141900Z` completed one exact-session Terra turn in 23.108 seconds, emitted zero problem records, and classified all three cleanup observations `unresolved` because deletion eligibility, retention policy, physical identities, storage impact, and one run's terminal context were absent. Finalization then failed on `problem_mining_final_non_support_case_present` because each atom still carried provisional `case:732fc6ff8fe54cface57`. A direct next-cycle replay proved that merely clearing those atom fields is insufficient: `build_case_registry` unions the old atom mappings back in and `normalize_atom_lineage` turns all three back into `supports_case`.
- Required correction: make qualification retraction an explicit, hash-bound case-evidence transition. Remove only authenticated corrected non-support edges from active atom/case maps and evidence lists; retain unrelated and partial support; preserve the case node and audit history; when its last source evidence is retracted, use the existing `superseded` lifecycle with reason `qualification_evidence_retracted`. The receipt must bind the feedback, author response, controller manifest, source evidence receipt, prior revision/state, retracted atom disposition receipts, and resulting revision/state. Failed finalization must not persist a misleading registry.
- Current live evidence: the 22.188-second exact-session rerun classified all three atoms `unresolved`, emitted zero active cases, persisted one hash-valid retraction receipt, removed every active atom/problem/ticket/signature map, retained the historical case as revision 2 `superseded`, and produced zero Stage 2 decisions. Direct normalization of the saved atoms against the saved registry preserved all three as non-support. The corrected response did not explicitly restate the 2 to 72 to 98 retained-tag trend and left `revisit_when` null, but its rationales identify the missing eligibility, physical-identity, retention-budget, storage, and terminal evidence; independent semantic audit judged these limitations insufficient to discard a defensible zero-case result. The subsequent model-free replay independently verified the corrected Stage 1 receipt with zero errors, preserved all three unresolved/null projections, and proved the retraction receipt valid. The remaining closure boundary is preservation against the populated production registry and lifecycle reconciliation, not this corrected author result.
- Closure proof: full and partial retraction, unrelated-map preservation, tamper rejection, idempotence, and next-cycle replay tests pass. The exact retained reviewer correction survives independent final-receipt re-verification and its Stage 2 consequence without restoring the false provisional case. A model-free production-function replay then preserves all 23 populated cases and all 25/320/320/15/5 lookup-map entries with the identical canonical registry hash while all three corrected atoms remain unresolved and unbound. The source graph predates and omits the real mitigated maintenance case; reconciling that separate lifecycle input is tracked by BDS-075 rather than left hidden inside this retraction defect.

This closes structural non-damage only; the maintenance outcome is still absent.

Its authenticated `verified_runner_ticket_ref_v2` binding targeted the maintenance case with zero errors. Twelve implementation-derived atoms updated that parent from 3 to 15 evidence atoms and 0 to 12 derived atoms while the graph remained exactly 24 cases, no new identity was minted, state remained `mitigated`, and an immediate second replay was canonically identical. ### BDS-077 - Semantic-basis repair feedback contradicted the validator's tagged-object contract

- Status: `closed_feedback_contract_verified_live`
- Priority: Stage 3 throughput-blocking, systemic
- Objective impact: a substantively correct dossier can converge to one structural finding and then spend repeated signed-in model turns rewording valid evidence because the author is not told the required JSON shape. This is controller-induced zero throughput, not evidence that the research is shallow.
- Exact evidence: the one-case Terra frontier for `case:131290268e2889891e0c` established the historical warning-overclassification mechanism and current `already_addressed` disposition. Across 12 executions in one author session, the validation-error frontier was `3,2,3,2,1,1,1,3,1,1,1,1`; 11 correction turns consumed 1,222.758 seconds after the 765.434-second investigation. The controller paused after the final three consecutive nonadvancing corrections and retained the authored frontier. The remaining `research_dossier_proof_adapter_semantic_basis_invalid` repeated while candidates used the guidance-shaped wrapper form such as `{"repository_contract_quote": {...}}`. The actual validator in `stage_contracts.py` requires `positive_outcome.semantic_basis` to be a mapping with a nonempty top-level `kind`. The repair prompt supplied only the opaque error code and generic instruction to correct it.
- Root cause: Stage 3 guidance described semantic bases as `repository_contract_quote={...}` and related forms without stating they are flat tagged objects. The generic remediation mapper had no branch for this exact validator code, so the same author could not infer that the missing field was `semantic_basis.kind`.
- Correction: guidance now shows flat objects with top-level `kind`, and the deterministic remediation hint names the exact field plus a concrete repository-contract example. It distinguishes exact source values, same-command fail-first proof, authenticated repository contracts, and source-cited interpreted expectations; tells the author to preserve a selector/predicate pair that already matches retained output; prohibits wrapper forms; and does not authorize new evidence or weaken validation. Six focused prompt/hint tests and Ruff pass.

### BDS-085 - A bare known reference could masquerade as counterevidence and authorize a weaker conclusion

- Status: `closed_component_and_final_preflight`
- Priority: root-cause quality and bad-throughput prevention, attempt-15 release-blocking
- Objective impact: cross-run objective-best persistence correctly blocks an unsupported weaker result, but its explicit evidence-backed-revision exception is too permissive. A model can add an existing support artifact or experiment ID to `counterevidence`/`disposition_evidence`, have the reference resolve, and cause the controller to stop treating the status change as an advancement regression. A verifier-clean `insufficient_evidence` candidate could then replace an evidence-sufficient objective best without proof that the original causal conclusion became untenable.
- Exact evidence: `_epistemic_downgrade_basis` currently forms one set of every after-dossier experiment/artifact ID and treats a newly listed matching reference as `hypothesis_counterevidence_added`; the existing positive test uses only `{"artifact_id":"artifact:new-counterevidence"}` with no refuting predicate, control relationship, or causal polarity. `_unsupported_substantive_coverage_loss` then suppresses lost-coverage findings, and the accepted path can promote the weaker result.
- Required correction: a counterevidence downgrade must resolve to an independently verified refuting control experiment, not a bare artifact or support experiment. A newly plausible/unresolved alternative may use a newly added or materially changed supporting experiment that is cited by that hypothesis as both supporting and disposition evidence. Preserve honest evidence-backed revision; reject reference relabeling. Prove the rule through the full correction acceptance path, not only a helper function.
- Closure proof: `_epistemic_downgrade_basis` now ignores bare artifacts and support-experiment relabeling. New counterevidence must be a newly cited `scenario_kind=control`, `outcome=refutes` experiment with a control relationship; a newly plausible/unresolved alternative must cite a new or materially changed `outcome=supports` experiment in both supporting and disposition evidence. The full candidate verifier still independently authenticates the experiment before acceptance. Bare-artifact and existing-support acceptance-path negatives remain nonaccepted; refuting-control and new-alternative positives remain eligible. Ten independently rerun focused cases pass. The complete affected files pass 160/160 in 141.462968 seconds under the 86,400-second ceiling; Ruff and diff checks pass. Final zero-model attempt-15 preflight v3 at `.tmp\component_validation\stage3_attempt15_preflight_v3_20260716T202222Z` passes in 0.111013 seconds with unchanged objective/correction/feedback hashes, no `stage_runs`, and no downstream/Docker action.

### BDS-087 - Attempt-15 feedback named the right evidence but omitted the executable proof contract

- Status: `case_specific_closed_general_feedback_generation_followup`
- Priority: real-case throughput and supervisor-intervention reduction
- Objective impact: attempt 15 followed the supplied semantic direction and reached the correct bounded conclusion, yet the feedback did not tell the author how the verifier authenticates either a direct aggregate-atom symptom binding or a transitive production mechanism. The result resolved all three inherited identities but produced ten cascading findings from three proof clusters. Repeating the same generic advice would waste another turn and incorrectly attribute supervisor guidance defects to model nonprogress.
- Exact run evidence: `U:\usertest_backlog_qualification\e0527e23_20260716T083600Z\component_frontiers\stage3_attempt15_continuation_20260716T202605Z\stage3_attempt15_receipt.json` records one same-author Terra turn, 318.36857 seconds total, ten post-turn findings, host-subscription provenance, and zero downstream/Docker calls. The authored report SHA-256 `EB9315C8...` correctly concludes `evidence_sufficient` plus code/test-level `already_addressed` with live verification unresolved. Independent audit reduces the raw ten identities to three real proof clusters: origin observation binding, authenticated mechanism traversal, and falsification outcome binding.
- Guidance error 1: attempt-15 feedback said to use `role=symptom`, the exact atom `field_path`, and `value=codex_model_messages_missing`. That verifies the snapshot scalar but does not make it a direct symptom binding when the experiment's observed stdout is only `[100%]`. The general contract also requires an `observation_predicate` evaluated against the atom value, or an observed assertion that contains the scalar. The omitted predicate caused one direct binding failure plus the two aggregate coverage cascades.
- Guidance error 2: feedback said the two existing `run_once` pytest selectors were sufficient as the baseline/challenge for survived falsification. They prove the end-to-end classifications, but the causal-control verifier authenticates direct/reachable calls from the selected test or an attested research helper. Those tests call `run_once` transitively and do not directly call `_sanitize_agent_stderr_text`; their quiet pytest stdout also does not itself expose `runtime_notice`. The omission caused the mechanism, causal-root, falsification, and survived-falsification cascades.
- Attempt-16 result: the same Terra author implemented the requested direct harness and retained all five useful experiments. All five replay with exit 0 and their declared assertions pass. The candidate still correctly concludes `evidence_sufficient` and code/test-level `already_addressed`, explicitly withholding live resolution. The 981.917881-second outer run used two same-session CLI calls (one substantive response plus one automatic schema-envelope repair), no downstream stage, and no Docker. Its 11 raw verifier findings were initially distorted by BDS-088; a post-fix 345.253464-second model-free replay leaves ten raw findings that reduce to four authored proof/attestation clusters plus two semantic mapping corrections, not ten independent research failures.
- Guidance correction: the prior instruction to add an equality `observation_predicate` was itself incomplete. A generic predicate is evaluated against the immutable atom scalar, but it becomes a runner-attested causal root only through a proof adapter that binds it to an observed baseline value. This direct Python harness already prints `code=codex_model_messages_missing`, so its simpler valid root is the ordinary symptom binding without a generic predicate: the observed key/value assertion directly binds the atom scalar. The history audit and runtime-only regression are ancillary current/history evidence, not independent direct reproductions of the aggregate symptom; they must not claim direct aggregate support merely to satisfy shape.
- Required correction: continue from the retained attempt-16 authored dossier and exact same session. Return only the independent delta: classify or bind the two ancillary experiments honestly; remove the generated overlay harness from baseline `inspected_files`; authenticate a narrow read containing the `run_once` definition rather than an unauthenticated 4,569-line read; make the direct sanitizer baseline a runner-rooted scalar symptom observation; connect the primary hypothesis's historical/current disposition to the history and `run_once` evidence without pretending those ancillary experiments are direct causal roots; and remove the owned-warning baseline from the external-runtime hypothesis's `counterevidence` because it does not contradict that alternative. Preserve the five successful experiments, correct bounded disposition, and live boundary.
- System follow-up: correction feedback should be generated from validator predicates/examples rather than hand-written summaries that can omit required fields. This must stay general: describe accepted proof capabilities and causal obligations, not this case's exact answer.
- Closure proof: attempt 17 continued from attempt 16 in the same author session, preserved the correct bounded disposition, and the corrected independent replay verified the authored dossier with no material proof finding and no Stage 4 work. The general follow-up remains to generate future correction feedback from validator capabilities/predicates rather than hand-written summaries; that nonblocking controller improvement should be processed from the ledger without reopening this case's research.

- Status: `closed_local_regression_and_real_replay`

It invoked zero models, downstream stages, or Docker.
- Correction: Python source read for AST verification now uses `utf-8-sig` at all seven verifier parse sites. PowerShell read attestation also decodes with `utf-8-sig`, treating the BOM as encoding metadata rather than unobserved source text. Focused regressions cover both paths.
- Verification: the two direct BOM regressions pass; five neighboring PowerShell read/range tests pass in 0.846849 seconds; eight neighboring attested-research pytest tests pass in 14.938068 seconds. The full affected files completed with 203 passed, one skipped, and zero failed in 121.119935 seconds; Ruff passed in 0.820311 seconds and `git diff --check` passed in 0.983554 seconds. An earlier sandboxed run is explicitly excluded because 30 temporary local-Git-clone tests hit sandbox access denial; the identical unsandboxed rerun is the valid result. The real replay proves the AST defect is gone, but also disproves the earlier expectation that only four raw errors would remain: a separate primary-support/root mapping problem produces six dependent findings. That is retained as focused same-author feedback under BDS-087 rather than being mislabeled as BOM failure.
- Closure proof: the full affected test files and Ruff pass; a UTF-8-BOM research harness produces the same verified mechanism/falsification receipts as its no-BOM equivalent; PowerShell full-file attestation observes identical logical content; and a real retained replay no longer emits an AST-invalid or unobserved-content finding solely because of the BOM.

### BDS-089 - Corrected evaluator findings were unioned with known-false source findings

When every binding verifies, the replacement list becomes the immediate feedback frontier without inventing a model attempt or carrying obsolete findings. Any missing, changed, or unavailable binding falls back to the existing additive union behavior.

The two existing additive independent-feedback cases also pass unchanged. The complete `test_research_runner.py` suite passes 141/141 in 111.602793 seconds; Ruff passes in 0.253921 seconds and `git diff --check` in 0.066601 seconds. ### BDS-090 - Stage continuation receipt undercounted internal self-healing calls

- Status: `closed_terminal_accounting_verified`

Every counted call must retain the expected session and requested Terra model.
- Verification: the superseding zero-model preflight at `.tmp\component_validation\stage3_attempt17_preflight_20260716T223227Z\stage3_attempt17_receipt.json` passed in 0.098699 seconds with one outer-turn limit, self-healing explicitly enabled, model count marked not requested, and no upstream, downstream, or Docker invocation. Helper `--help` import and Ruff both pass. The 710.204035-second attempt-17 terminal receipt binds `agent_attempts.json` SHA-256 `e95b106ac2038edd096d7943a87cb7f636b3f4933a351534e7abef5b4c05e0ac` and correctly reports one model call, zero same-session follow-ups, and zero transient retries.

### BDS-091 - Direct-coverage guard rejected an honest verifier-requested evidence-role correction

- Status: `closed_local_and_real_replay_pending_canonical_import`
- Priority: current-case disposition blocking, evaluator correctness
- Objective impact: attempt 17 cleared all ten evidence-verifier findings and retained a substantively sufficient root-cause analysis, yet the progression guard quarantined it and requested that the same author restore direct coverage the verifier had explicitly rejected. - Exact evidence: the 710.204035-second attempt-17 receipt records one signed-in Terra call, no downstream/Docker work, and `repairable_paused:repair_turn_limit_reached`. The sole rejection is fourteen `origin_atom[...].direct_experimental_coverage` losses caused by changing the history audit from `supports` to `inconclusive`. Independent audit confirms these are fourteen manifestations of one evaluator contradiction, not fourteen author defects.
- Root cause: representative-aggregate evidence verification intentionally requires a direct binding only for the aggregate atom and treats occurrence IDs as lineage. `_substantive_research_coverage` separately projected every `addresses_atom_ids` entry from any `supports`/`refutes` experiment as durable direct coverage, even when the current verifier emitted `experiment_not_bound_to_atom` for that experiment. The guard therefore preserved a false proof role more strongly than the evidence verifier's authenticated result.
- Correction: retain the substantive-loss guard for causal, mechanism, outcome, and unrelated atom coverage. When the current verifier explicitly rejects an experiment's direct atom binding and the author responds by changing the experiment-wide outcome away from `supports`/`refutes`, treat only direct-atom roles contributed solely by that rejected experiment as an evidence-backed revision. An unrelated binding error, silent atom-list deletion while support remains claimed, or loss of hypothesis/mechanism coverage still fails closed.
- Verification: five focused tests pass in 4.034870 seconds, covering the representative aggregate/occurrence correction, unrelated-error rejection, silent atom deletion, retained hypothesis/mechanism protection, and the existing repeated substantive-regression/recovery paths. The complete research-runner suite passes 144/144 in 162.738332 seconds; Ruff and `git diff --check` pass. The isolated model-free replay at `U:\usertest_backlog_qualification\e0527e23_20260716T083600Z\component_frontiers\stage3_attempt17_progression_replay_20260716T231401Z` re-evaluated the exact retained candidate, independently replayed all five experiments, and persisted `repair_contract_valid`, evidence-verification SHA-256 `60cc0996a9e95b2a51f6e8b16a015c28196529fa8d1832644fac5d2c6f0cf61a`, and zero validation errors. It used zero model, downstream, or Docker calls and kept the source manifest unchanged.
- Closure proof: the complete research-runner suite passes and the unchanged retained candidate is re-evaluated by the corrected controller, independently verifies its experiments, persists `repair_contract_valid` with zero errors, and reaches the code/test-only already-addressed disposition without a new model turn.
