### BDS-021 - Single-path causal work was rejected as unsupported shared-abstraction breadth

- Status: `closed_for_current_case_monitoring_generalization`
- Priority: objective-blocking, throughput-degrading
- Objective impact: Stage 5 asked the option author to remove pre-resolution cleanup and caller-specific protection because the option had only one runner-evidenced scope path. That would preserve the surface-level failure this remediation is intended to prevent: a locally correct cleanup predicate placed after the real operation had already exhausted storage.
- Exact evidence: the first selector in `stage4_maintenance_lifecycle_rework_20260715T020353Z` returned `option_revision_requested` and said to separate the cleanup-helper change from resolver/manual extensions because `scope_evidence.scope_level=single_path`. Repository inspection instead shows `run_batch_preflight` executes a storage-writing `docker buildx build --load` before calling `resolve_maintenance_docker_image`; resolver-only cleanup cannot recover the batch after that earlier failure. The option did not claim a new class-wide abstraction: it extended an existing cleanup helper and its existing callers along one reachable maintenance operation.
- Root cause: the two-independent-path guard for new shared abstractions and class-wide claims was applied to the number of files/callers touched by one causal path. The selector therefore optimized for narrow PR surface rather than complete root-cause coverage.
- Local correction: generic and internal Stage 4/5 prompts and guidance now reserve the two-path rule for broad problem claims and newly introduced reusable abstractions. A single evidenced operation may cross existing components, extend an existing helper, and require sequencing/protection changes. Selector/falsifier guidance now requires locating the earliest same-resource failure boundary, preserving safe useful throughput after partial auxiliary failures, and avoiding benchmark/default values as unsupported universal maxima.
- Verification: 81 relevant planning, Stage-5 self-healing, policy-boundary, and causal-scope tests passed with the 24-hour ceiling; Ruff and diff checks passed. In the real supervised rerun, the exact original selector session corrected its critique, identified the pre-scratch-build boundary, partial-cleanup throughput rule, configured-count invariant, full current-identity protection, and post-cleanup inventory requirement. That corrected feedback was delivered to the exact original option-author session, which produced a materially revised option.
- Closure proof: met for the current case. Continue monitoring sealed/general cases for false breadth rejection without weakening the genuine two-independent-path gate for new shared/class-wide claims.

### BDS-022 - Optional Stage 3 baseline contract is treated as the exclusive future-solution gate

- Status: `closed_for_current_case_monitoring_generalization`
- Priority: objective-blocking, systemic
- Objective impact: an otherwise strong option cannot reach planning because Stage 5 requires an immutable pre-change research contract to prove the future implementation's end-to-end recovery. Stage 4 cannot repair that contract, so same-author correction loops on an impossible request or downgrades valid work.
- Exact evidence: in `stage5_maintenance_lifecycle_same_selector_20260715T022239Z`, the original option author revised the solution to run bounded identity cleanup before the batch scratch build and direct resolver writes, verify actual Docker inventory, preserve the complete current identity, and require successful scratch-build-plus-resolution continuation. The selector provisionally accepted it. The falsifier ultimately rejected only because retained `positive_outcome_contract:5bcbc293...` proves the Stage 3 fresh-burst identity-count baseline and cannot already observe the future pre-scratch-build implementation. The option's `causal_coverage.outcome_strategy` already names every demanded future observation. A second option revision made that strategy even more explicit, but the loaded validator still routed the immutable Stage 3 contract back to the option author.
- Root cause: `bind_falsification_review` chooses mutually exclusive paths: it reviews the Stage 4 outcome strategy only when research has no positive contracts; when any legacy research contract exists, it requires that contract to be sufficient post-change proof. This conflates current-mechanism evidence with prospective solution semantics and contradicts the Stage 3/4/5/6 boundary.
- Required correction: always review and content-address the selected option's Stage 4 outcome strategy. Treat retained Stage 3 contracts as optional baseline/additional evidence. A surface-only research baseline must not block selection when the Stage 4 strategy would prove intended operation; a surface-only Stage 4 strategy must still block. Stage 6 must convert the accepted strategy into exact executable checks, and implementation/outcome execution must supply the actual post-change proof.
- Supervision disposition: the live run was stopped without deleting artifacts when it began a second falsifier cycle under the uncorrectable loaded contract. The strongest same-author revised option and selector acceptance are retained and will be used as the next Stage 5 input after the systemic boundary is fixed; Stage 3 and Stage 4 will not be restarted.
- Closure proof: focused and affected suites pass; Stage 5 accepts the retained strong option while accurately labeling the research baseline contract as partial/surface-only; the selected Stage 5 outcome contract is content-addressed from the sufficient Stage 4 strategy; and Stage 6 receives that strategy for exact post-change verification.
- Live closure evidence: `stage5_maintenance_outcome_boundary_replay_20260715T025512Z` selected `option:pre-resolution-bounded-identity-cleanup-with-contextual-protection`. The independent falsifier accepted the prospective Stage 4 outcome strategy as sufficient with full problem coverage after three same-session mechanical corrections. The bound record is `outcome_contract_status=approved_for_planning` and `post_change_evidence_status=unverified`; all six model/auth manifests verify the signed-in host ChatGPT subscription route with API fallback disabled. This advances the case to Stage 6 without claiming implementation or resolution.

### BDS-023 - Terminating the shell wrapper did not terminate the live stage descendant tree

- Status: `open`
- Priority: operational, supervision-integrity
- Objective impact: a supervisor can believe a pathological correction loop is stopped while the Python controller and Codex descendants continue consuming subscription capacity and writing new artifacts. Metrics and handoff state then drift after the declared cutoff.
- Exact evidence: the wrapper cell for `stage5_maintenance_lifecycle_same_selector_20260715T022239Z` reported `Script terminated`, but the original `run_stages456_one.py` Python process (PID 45316), its Python 3.13 child (PID 21180), and a later Terra Codex child continued. The orphaned controller completed a third option revision at 02:42:31Z and started another selector/falsifier cycle. At 02:42:58Z the live tree was identified by exact output-root command line and explicitly stopped (Codex PID 16592, Python PIDs 21180 and 45316). No artifact was deleted or rewritten.
- Required action: stage cancellation must terminate and await the complete process tree, then persist a cancellation receipt containing descendant PIDs, final artifact frontier, and whether any post-request writes occurred. Metrics must use the actual final filesystem frontier, not the earlier wrapper cutoff.
- Current disposition: include the post-cutoff third author revision and subsequent partial cycle as orphaned retained evidence; do not count that orphaned cycle as completed. A separate clean replay, `stage5_maintenance_outcome_boundary_replay_20260715T025512Z`, started from the strongest authenticated option revision and completed Stage 5. BDS-023 remains open only for general process-tree cancellation correctness.
- Closure proof: a focused long-running child fixture is cancelled; all descendants exit; no later artifact timestamps appear; and the cancellation receipt matches the observed process tree.

### BDS-024 - Selected retention outcome is bounded above but does not forbid over-deletion

- Status: `planning_contract_closed_implementation_proof_pending`
- Priority: solution-correctness, implementation-blocking
- Objective impact: an implementation that deletes every ordinary unprotected maintenance image could satisfy the selected `ordinary identities <= keep_local_count` property and recover capacity while violating the intended retention behavior. That would replace the original accumulation failure with destructive over-cleaning.
- Exact evidence: the selected Stage 5 contract in `stage5_maintenance_outcome_boundary_replay_20260715T025512Z` requires ordinary unprotected identities to remain at or below `cfg.keep_local_count`, actual post-cleanup Docker reclamation, and successful constrained continuation. It does not explicitly require retaining the newest `min(cfg.keep_local_count, eligible ordinary identity count)` identities. Its `original_scenario_experiment_ids` contains only `experiment:pytest-fresh-source-bound`; the authenticated `experiment:pytest-initial-single-hash` observation is not carried as a below-budget compatibility scenario.
- Disposition: do not restart Stage 3 or discard the accepted option. Give the Stage 6 planner this exact gap and require an executable compatibility check that a below-budget inventory is unchanged plus a multi-identity assertion that the newest in-budget ordinary identities remain while only overflow identities are removed. The plan must carry this alongside the existing capacity-recovery, current/protected identity, external-tag/container blocker, and partial-error controls.
- Current proof: the final Stage 6 plan at `stage6_maintenance_image_decision_complete_20260715T123600Z/change_plans.json` requires a below-budget unchanged replay and newest-first retention of exactly `min(keep_local_count, eligible ordinary identity count)` during overflow. The independent semantic audit found no remaining over-deletion loophole.
- Closure proof: the planning half is met. Implementation and post-change outcome execution must still pass both checks before this problem may be called mitigated in operation.

### BDS-025 - Stage 6 requires future post-change live proof before it may plan that proof

- Status: `closed_for_current_case_monitoring_generalization`
- Priority: objective-blocking, systemic
- Objective impact: a causally sufficient case and adversarially approved outcome strategy are returned to research because the future implementation's exact live command has not already run before implementation. This makes a decision-complete plan impossible and drives the same kind of zero-throughput loop that kept Stage 3 open after attempt 19.
- Exact evidence: `stage6_maintenance_image_stage5_final_terra_20260715T032500Z` received the verifier-clean Stage 3 dossier and the final Stage 5 selection `option:pre-resolution-bounded-identity-cleanup-with-contextual-protection`, inspected the repository, and then emitted zero plans with `planning_status=research_required`. Its stated blocker was the absence of a runner-verified live Docker experiment for the future capacity-recovery behavior. The planning prompt simultaneously says Stage 6 owns exact after-change commands and predicates grounded in Stage 3 baseline evidence, Stage 5 semantics, and inspected source, but later requires every live command to bind an exact research experiment that already ran that command. Those requirements are circular for a new solution.
- Required correction: retain the authenticated Stage 3 before-state replay and Stage 5 outcome contract. Permit Stage 6 to define a new exact post-change live command, predicates, repository touchpoints, and harness integration from those inputs while keeping its execution state explicitly `unverified`. Require pre-existing research binding for the original/before scenario and for any claim about current behavior; require implementation/outcome execution before the new live command can support `tests_verified`, `live_verified`, or `resolved`. Return to research only when root cause, interface choice, change surface, or required outcome semantics remain materially undecided.
- Current execution boundary: continue the same planner session `019f63d1-90ba-7b41-bf2c-4e9b76d3ad0b` with this exact feedback after the systemic contract is fixed. Do not rerun Stages 3-5 and do not replace the author merely because its first answer followed contradictory instructions.
- Current proof: the same planner session produced plan revision `planrev:sha256:ed4d010e810deab419cffd90b5804ca892f19dd48b4570ea1be2ed657975c725`. Its future isolated-daemon checks are explicitly `planned_unverified`, the maximum outcome is `mitigated`, and neither research nor selection was rerun. Planning and ticket-readiness validation pass without treating the unexecuted future command as existing evidence.
- Closure proof: met for the current case. Keep the distinction under monitoring on future plans; implementation and live execution remain separate gates.

### BDS-026 - Stage 6 pauses on cumulative correction cost after material progress

- Status: `closed_for_current_case_monitoring_generalization`
- Priority: throughput-blocking, systemic
- Objective impact: the planner can reduce a large mechanical error set to a small repairable frontier, then be paused because accumulated correction time exceeds the first response cost. This treats elapsed effort as stronger evidence than objective progress and strands good authored work instead of correcting it.
- Exact evidence: in `stage6_maintenance_image_same_planner_continuation_20260715T033500Z`, exact planner session `019f63d1-90ba-7b41-bf2c-4e9b76d3ad0b` reduced validator findings from 37 to 8 to 2. The two-error response is `implementation_planning_001_correction_002.response.txt`, SHA-256 `3744d8c2f5edc844630f2ea258c45075a4b15b800857a92930af916ccd89e4a6`. Two later replies regressed, after which the controller returned `repairable_paused:correction_cost_reached_original` because 700.14 correction seconds exceeded the 258.90-second initial response. The best frontier and same author remain available; there is no evidence that its two remaining issues are uncorrectable or equivalent to restarting.
- Required correction: preserve the objective-best valid-item/error frontier independently of the latest response and continue the same author from that frontier when later turns regress. Error-count reduction or valid-item increase is progress regardless of error identity. Cumulative cost alone must not pause a materially improved, low-error frontier. A pause remains appropriate only after repeated non-advancing work from the same best frontier, an uncorrectable external condition, or rework demonstrably equivalent to restart.
- Current execution boundary: do not start a new planner or rerun Stages 3-5. Authenticate the exact two-error response, classify those two findings, and return only legitimate corrections plus any substantive independent depth finding to the same session.
- Current proof: elapsed correction cost is now telemetry rather than a stop condition. The exact planner session continued from the retained frontier through the mechanically green four-turn run and then received the independent semantic findings. It corrected those findings in two additional authenticated turns rather than being restarted or discarded. The final two-turn correction used about 301 seconds end to end (290.03 seconds of recorded model-turn time).
- Closure proof: met for the current case and focused controller regressions. Continue monitoring nondeterministic cases for genuine three-turn material nonprogress rather than reintroducing an elapsed-time cutoff.

### BDS-027 - Mechanically valid Stage 6 plan leaves core interfaces and live proof architecture undecided

- Status: `closed_at_planning_gate_implementation_review_pending`
- Priority: solution-depth, implementation-blocking
- Objective impact: a plan can pass structural readiness while leaving the implementer to design the cross-module API, cleanup-result semantics, and live capacity harness. That would recreate the historical failure mode where detailed prose masks unresolved implementation and proof decisions.
- Exact evidence: planner attempt 4 (`implementation_planning_001_correction_003.response.txt`, SHA-256 `a189f4b519f04d35c7279b49955bb7f56413aebd069c7b5224c58a617eea2bde`) is mechanically ready after correcting the pytest `-p no:cacheprovider` parser false positive. It correctly addresses the count-or-age root cause and BDS-024, but independent repository-grounded review found three material gaps. First, it does not name the prepared-identity type/function, exact fields, resolver handoff parameter, cleanup protection input, or cleanup-result/artifact schema; the batch target omits the existing `run_batch_preflight` symbol, and removal of `keep_local_days` does not name `MaintenanceDockerConfig` or `_load_maintenance_docker_config`. Second, the new isolated-daemon test does not specify daemon provisioning, storage-capacity control, subprocess routing, host-state isolation, or baseline/recovery inventory equivalence, and its predicates do not prove baseline failure, blocker preservation, safe partial state, failed-continuation blocking, or manual-cleanup behavior. Third, it reuses the aged Stage 3 causal control as a recurrence probe even though recurrence belongs to the centralized two-shadow-cycle check and age grace is intentionally removed.
- Disposition: preserve the exact same planner session, mechanism, targets, before/after scenario, and non-over-deletion requirements. Return one focused Stage 6 correction that chooses exact APIs/data flow and status fields; makes the live harness and paired predicates executable; leaves recurrence empty/centralized; and clarifies whether the `.usertest_research` replay is repository material or outcome-runner materialization. This is planning work, not a return to Stage 3.
- Current proof: the final plan names the prepared-identity owner and fields, every cleanup phase, ordered operation attempts and exception-path persistence, exact batch and resolver control points, server-owned replay staging, the reachable loopback isolated-daemon architecture, arm-qualified paired predicates, and centralized recurrence. The content-addressed independent audit `stage6_maintenance_decision_complete_semantic_audit_20260715.json` records all three prior material findings resolved, zero remaining material defects, and `ready_for_supervised_implementation`.
- Closure proof: met at the planning gate. The audit retains two nonblocking implementation-review checks—failed-attempt evidence must remain reachable after exceptions, and each isolated pair must assert its own daemon endpoint and alias survival. Those are implementation conformance checks, not reasons to reopen research or planning.

### BDS-028 - Immutable Stage 5 causal coverage is regenerated as planner prose

- Status: `closed`

All other causal-coverage fields are identical. The validator returned only `change_plan_causal_coverage_linkage_mismatch`; corrections 5 and 6 retained that same sole error, and correction 6 was byte-identical to correction 5.
- Required correction: bind immutable selected-option causal coverage and scope evidence server-side when finalizing a Stage 6 candidate, preserving the exact Stage 5 objects and their provenance. If any remaining immutable linkage is model-authored, validator feedback must identify the exact differing JSON pointer and expected value rather than a generic mismatch.

### BDS-029 - Stage 6 requires a plan-authored recurrence command for centralized recurrence

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: objective-correctness, throughput-blocking
- Objective impact: recurrence is owned by the pipeline's two-shadow-cycle case refresh, but Stage 6 validation requires nonempty recurrence commands and predicates. The planner is forced to misclassify a Stage 3 causal replay as operational recurrence, undermining the outcome model and generating avoidable corrections.
- Exact evidence: the bound Stage 6 feedback explicitly required empty plan-authored recurrence commands/predicates and centralized ownership. The planner's correction 2 followed that boundary and received `missing_outcome_role_commands:recurrence`. Later corrections added the below-budget research replay solely to satisfy the validator. That replay verifies compatibility at the recorded revision; it does not establish absence of recurrence across future shadow cycles.
- Required correction: represent centralized recurrence with an explicit machine-verifiable ownership marker and allow empty plan-authored commands/predicates only for that ownership mode. Continue to require and validate commands/predicates when recurrence is genuinely owned by the plan.
- Current proof: the final plan uses `verification_owner=centralized_case_refresh` with empty recurrence commands and predicates, explicitly assigning recurrence to two later shadow-cycle refreshes. Plan-owned recurrence remains command-validated in focused negative tests.
- Closure proof: met.

### BDS-030 - Stage 4 selected an old-behavior assertion as the post-change original-scenario replay

- Status: `closed_for_current_case`
- Priority: outcome-correctness, implementation-blocking
- Objective impact: a plan can claim an exact original-scenario binding while replacing the retained test asset that defined that experiment. The resulting after-change command is no longer the authenticated research experiment, and outcome materialization can collide with a repository-created file at the same destination.
- Exact evidence: the selected Stage 4/5 outcome strategy names `experiment:pytest-fresh-source-bound`. Its command runs `.usertest_research/test_maintenance_image_burst_replay.py::test_fresh_burst_retains_source_symptom`, exits 0 before the change, and asserts that all 49 identities remain. The Stage 6 plan then proposes creating/replacing that same path with post-change assertions while retaining the research binding. `runner_core.outcome_roles` rejects an existing retained-asset destination as `outcome_oracle_materialization_collision`. The same Stage 3 dossier already contains `experiment:fresh-burst-contract-fail-first`, whose unchanged command exits 1 before the fix because it asserts the intended bounded behavior and is designed to pass after the change.
- Required correction: distinguish authenticated before-state observations from commands that are safe to replay unchanged as post-change proof. Prefer a verified fail-first or explicitly post-change-replay-eligible experiment when available. When none exists, permit an honest `planned_unverified` alternate integrated through inspected repository boundaries without pretending it is the exact research command; do not create a new circular Stage 3 execution requirement for the future solution.
- Current execution boundary: correct the option through the same optioner session, then rerun the same selector/falsifier against the revised immutable option contract before returning to Stage 6. Do not restart Stage 3.
- Current proof: one authenticated Stage 4 correction in optioner session `019f6384-3b6b-7503-983a-71f8e3dbdfa7` replaced the old-behavior selector with `experiment:fresh-burst-contract-fail-first`. Stage 5 reselected and falsified that exact contract. Stage 6 now uses the unchanged content-addressed retained asset, requires exit 1 with retained count 49 before implementation and exit 0 with retained count 5 afterward, and introduces no `.usertest_research` change target.
- Closure proof: met for selection and planning. The unchanged after-state replay has now passed after each of six system-authored implementation commits, with retained count 5 at configured bound 5. Live Docker, recurrence, and durable OutcomeRecord evidence remain absent.

### BDS-031 - Falsifier prompt and validator required different evidence and residual-risk shapes

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: throughput-degrading, self-healing correctness
- Objective impact: a substantively useful falsifier could be rejected for following the written contract, then spend correction turns rediscovering a schema rule instead of testing the proposed mechanism.

The prompt instead described `mechanism_evidence_id` refs and did not state the exact-string residual binding. The resulting findings were formatting/contract mismatches rather than evidence that the selected mechanism was shallow.

The direct root diagnostic is returned to the same falsifier rather than being hidden behind downstream findings.

### BDS-034 - Structural Stage 6 readiness accepted a semantically incomplete plan

### BDS-035 - UTF-8 BOM made an existing Python target symbol appear missing

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: mechanics, throughput-degrading
- Objective impact: the planner could name the correct `run_batch_preflight` symbol and still receive a false missing-symbol error, consuming same-author corrections unrelated to solution quality.
- Exact evidence: the clean revision's `apps/usertest_implement/src/usertest_implement/batch_preflight.py` begins with U+FEFF. `_python_target_bindings` passed those bytes directly to `ast.parse`, caught the resulting syntax error, and returned no bindings. The Stage 6 validator then reported the real symbol as unresolved.
- Local correction: symbol grounding strips one leading UTF-8 BOM before parsing while still rejecting genuine syntax errors. The focused BOM-positive and syntax-error-negative cases are included in 69 passing symbol/planning tests.
- Closure proof: the corrected validator resolves the existing batch-preflight symbol, and the final plan binds it successfully. This changes only source parsing; it does not relax target existence or symbol checks.

### BDS-036 - Ticket exporter and runtime outcome-role validation reject the server-bound fail-first replay accepted by Stage 6

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: implementation/export blocking, contract-integrity
- Objective impact: the exact plan passes Stage 6 and ticket readiness but cannot enter the normal ticket/implementation path. Treating that as a model or planning failure would rerun correct authored work; bypassing the exporter would hide a real integration break.

Ordinary staged, causal, and multi-scenario replay roles still require their research positive-contract coverage. The Stage 5 contract ID and repository revision now remain available to the live and mitigation bindings for this server-bound form.

Export `maintenance_image.tickets_export.json` has SHA-256 `423e8af26029e122306e0d7fab46471419f5bb08a94b31e9840307b15c8a4aa6`; materialization receipt SHA-256 is `1d49220de6e346f255e1cd79920d003fa33f5b8fecf3ad72c748750203829393`. The additive local plan has raw-file SHA-256 `dfe83820fc6c95dd227ea8ca9959b0dbc56c2a16151e861e7423a7cdc012bab8` and canonical local-plan SHA-256 `349351ebc6d4afe69369575eee77c7fbc1a93e6727078c04a36bfda0fb90be1f`; its verification and target contracts are SHA-256 `f3e3a62c1a2bd426ed81745e2dccc6c3414f1503ed2a2c24b8cc764112cb5c98` and `98ff1d11ffeea4f537cde3cd9b89cd5d6b393fbe6a9c61ea345cbe0f599278fb`. Exact target revision remains `51e35fbcb50151590604611f10910a7fd13660e4`. No existing ticket was moved or removed, no model stage was rerun, and no Docker action occurred.

### BDS-038 - One malformed shell rejection was misclassified as a read-only workspace

- Status: `mitigated_for_current_case_monitoring`
- Priority: throughput-degrading, self-healing correctness
- Objective impact: a correct implementation can produce zero code when the author treats a command-policy or quoting rejection as proof that the filesystem is read-only, then repeats the false premise instead of attempting a simple tracked edit.
- Exact evidence: the original implementation run `usertest/20260715T143437Z/codex/0` contains three exit-zero turns in session `019f6635-03a2-7f60-acab-3d97e277332f`, each reporting the workspace read-only and making no code change. Its recorded Codex argv and preflight explicitly say `sandbox=workspace-write` and policy allowed; the rejected PowerShell command is malformed/quote-corrupted, a simpler read succeeds, and no tracked-file write had been attempted. The same retained workspace/session later produced eight commits.
- Current correction: supervisor feedback returned the exact evidence to the same author and required one simple read plus a real tracked `apply_patch` attempt before any read-only claim. Good work was retained; the author was not restarted.
- Required systemic correction: classify filesystem capability from the effective sandbox contract plus an exact simple write attempt, not from one complex command rejection. Persist the exact rejection category and avoid repeating a disproven premise.
- Closure proof: the current case is recovered. A later independent implementation run must self-correct this condition without supervisor intervention before the finding can close generally.

### BDS-040 - Successful subscription-backed attempts are mislabeled `provider_auth`

### BDS-013 — Maintenance prospective outcome oracle proves deletion, not bounded convergence

- Status: `moved_downstream`
- Priority: optioning, selection, planning, and outcome blocking; not Stage 3 blocking
- Objective impact: a plan could pass a one-burst regression while still allowing unbounded accumulation over larger or repeated bursts, which would reproduce the original root problem.
- Evidence: the latest parameterized harness improves the fail-first breadth—actual retained counts of 9 and 13 both exceed a computed bound of 5—but it does not establish the claimed safety or convergence contract. It merely labels five ordinary image IDs current/protected/active/running/shared without supplying those signals to production cleanup; uses `|S|=5` in both cases while never passing `L` to the production policy; verifies alias preservation only while all IDs remain; prints `preserve_S`, deletion safety, overflow, and convergence as literal `true`; computes the same `min(M, bound)` twice instead of invoking cleanup twice; and checks only that result field names exist. Thus the only substantive executable result is the broad bounded-retention failure, not protected-identity safety or a fixed point.
- Current attempt-44 evidence: the author materially improved the oracle by invoking production cleanup over mutable state and measuring `M`, protected set `S`, retained `R`, deletion scope, a second pass, and a subsequent burst. It still hardcodes `keep_local_days=0`, so it can pass without fixing the observed one-day-under-seven-day-grace mechanism; records but does not assert post-burst safety/bound/deletion scope or a final convergence pass; claims three aliases while only two repositories exist; checks only image-ID sets and can leave a partial alias family; and calculates protected overflow without requiring it to be emitted. Cleanup-before-build exhaustion is separately reproduced but not yet modeled as a causal facet, so optioning could omit recovery ordering.
- Current live-repair evidence: the retained early dossier corrected the historical policy input to one-day identities under the seven-day grace and ran four cleanup transitions. Its outcome oracle is still materially insufficient. `REPOSITORIES` contains exactly two repositories, so the advertised `aliases=3` relation silently executes two aliases and the two relations are actually 2/2 rather than 1/2. The test asserts first-pass `S <= R`, first-pass deletion scope, first/second equality, and post-burst/final equality, but bounds only first-pass `R` and checks alias integrity only in the first summary. Retained replay output shows post-burst/final `R=12` for bound 5 and `R=16` for bound 6; equality of those unbounded states was mislabeled as convergence. The report's independent outcome observable remains only `exit_code == 1`, and the verifier correctly reports `production_assertion_dataflow_unresolved` because the selected test passes the production callable through a helper rather than binding an authenticated direct production-output assertion.
- Required action: preserve this semantic finding, but do not send the maintenance case back through Stage 3 for it. Stage 4 must propose success properties and safety constraints that address bounded repeated behavior; Stage 5 must reject options that merely delete something or hide the symptom; Stage 6 must author exact executable checks; the outcome stage must run them against the implementation. Protection categories not exercised by the current mechanism must be scoped honestly to live verification rather than printed as established.
- Closure proof: the selected option and final plan cover multiple burst sizes or repeated cycles and specify executable assertions that unique managed identities are bounded by the selected ceiling plus reported required overflow; aliases count per identity; protected/active/running/shared/non-owned identities survive; and overflow/convergence is observable. Actual closure occurs only when post-change outcome evidence passes those checks.

### BDS-015 — Causal-contrast predicates are conflated with operational outcome contracts

- Status: `stage_boundary_correction_in_progress`
- Priority: case-blocking, systemic
- Objective impact: a useful controlled difference is forced either to masquerade as the complete post-change success criterion or to fail proof validation. This rewards shallow predicates such as “fewer than 49” and prevents a general, independently executable outcome contract from coexisting with causal mechanism evidence.
- Evidence: `proof_adapters.base.build_receipt` evaluates `positive_outcome.predicate` against the challenge observation and stores it on the causal receipt. `_causal_proof_positive_contract` then unconditionally promotes that predicate into a post-change `causal_proof_predicate`. Yet `_positive_outcome_contracts` explicitly states that a different control input is causal boundary evidence, not by itself an original-input positive contract. For the maintenance case, the correct fresh/aged contrast yields the scalar `3`; the real success criterion is a relational, multi-cycle `M/S/L` property. Making the scalar predicate `< 49` would pass mechanics while remaining substantively shallow.
- Adversarial evidence retained under this issue rather than multiplied into new hardening tickets: an independent outcome experiment must not bootstrap the mechanism it claims to bind; `exit 0` alone can mean skip, xfail, zero collection, or an early return; and the existing monotonic AST taint accepts false flows such as `x=production(); x=0; assert x==1` or a production call in an untaken conditional. Printed literal booleans and a production call followed by an unrelated assertion are not outcome evidence. These are direct false-acceptance paths for shallow plans, not speculative PR-scope controls.
- Required action: retain the backward-compatible distinction between causal challenge and operational outcome, but move the mandatory future-solution contract out of Stage 3 readiness. A causal receipt must establish mechanism evidence without being forced to predict the final implementation. Stage 4 defines candidate success properties and safety constraints, Stage 5 reviews them adversarially, Stage 6 binds them to exact executable checks, and the outcome stage supplies post-change proof. A stronger research-authored operational contract may flow downstream when available, but it is not a universal prerequisite for optioning.
- Minimum authenticity boundary for the independent outcome: bind only to already-verified primary/root mechanism evidence that is independent of the outcome experiment; retain the exact failing assertion and call-to-observation-to-assertion path; accept only a deliberately restricted direct or immutable projection rather than the existing monotonic taint; reject overwrites, untaken conditionals, constant-dominated/reflexive/both-sides-tainted comparisons, unrelated failures, and production-symbol monkeypatching. Preserve exact selector/call-phase outcome telemetry so a later pass cannot be inferred from process exit alone. Do not require multiple scenarios generically: Stage 5 remains responsible for judging whether the authenticated assertion and scenario coverage are substantively sufficient for the problem.
- Local correction: `proof_adapter.positive_outcome.contract_role="causal_contrast"` is now an opt-in role. Its challenge predicate remains authenticated, runner-evaluated mechanism evidence but is not promoted as operational success; absence preserves legacy behavior. A separate `positive_outcome_contract.binds_hypothesis_id` path now mints a content-addressed runner receipt only when the outcome experiment is independent of mechanism selection, uses one exact retained pytest node, fails at the bound production-derived assertion before implementation, and has restricted unconditional single-assignment data flow without overwrite, constant domination, reflexive comparison, skip, xfail, parameterization, early return, or selected-symbol rebinding. Future closure remains explicitly `unverified_until_outcome_execution` until the same exact node exits zero and reports one pass. Full affected verification passed: 167 research-evidence tests plus one expected skip, 176 Stage-contract tests, 115 adjacent miner tests, 24 causal-proof tests, eight outcome-role tests plus one expected skip, focused compatibility tests, Ruff, and diff checks. This is deliberately a narrow authenticity boundary; Stage 5 still owns semantic sufficiency and scenario breadth.
- Closure proof: focused contract tests prove a contrast-only causal adapter can establish Stage 3 mechanism evidence without minting a post-change contract; legacy research outcome contracts remain compatible; and Stage 4-6 plus the outcome stage—not repeated Stage 3 turns—carry success semantics through exact planned and executed verification.

### BDS-009 — Repeated runs can accumulate Docker images and local workspaces

- Status: `operationally_recovered_root_cause_confirmed_monitoring_required`
- Priority: operational, pipeline-blocking if recurrence is not detected early
- Objective impact: Docker host-allocation growth exhausted the host system drive and prevented evidence replay, halting the pipeline and risking unrelated host operations.
- Pre-recovery evidence: at 14:53–15:00 America/New_York on 2026-07-14, C: had only 0.225–0.34 GiB free of 487.5 GiB. `C:\Users\jason\AppData\Local\Docker\wsl\disk\docker_data.vhdx` occupied 192.755 GiB on the host, while read-only inspection inside the mounted Docker disk found only about 5.8 GiB used. The retained usertest workspace root was 8.606 GiB logical, so workspace cleanup was not the primary explanation for the host-space incident.
- Object-retention evidence: the 2026-07-13 cleanup receipt records 31 stopped containers, 49 obsolete image IDs, and 2.426 GB of build cache removed. Immediately afterward Docker contained zero containers, four unique image IDs, 3.554 GB of reported image data, and zero build cache, but the VHDX still occupied exactly 206,968,979,456 bytes (192.755 GiB). The bounded live inventory therefore did not cause the later host-file size; deletion had made blocks free inside the guest without returning their allocation to C:.
- Churn evidence: retained BuildKit history contains 50 builds from 2026-07-08 through 2026-07-13; 44 are explicitly maintenance-named, 30 originated under `runs/usertest_implement`, and 46 report only seven cached steps. The current maintenance image includes a 1.67 GB dependency layer and a 1.69 GB source-dependent environment-seeding layer; its later workspace deletion is an 8.19 KB whiteout layer and does not erase lower-layer bytes. Windows VHDMP summaries record 75.786 GiB of writes on July 8, 139.092 GiB on July 9, 11.981 GiB on July 10, 34.738 GiB on July 11, 30.907 GiB on July 12, 2.810 GiB on July 13, and 0.467 GiB before compaction on July 14. These figures are I/O volume, not unique allocation, but they establish that repeated substantive image rebuilds were quantitatively capable of driving the high-water allocation and that most write pressure preceded the final two days.
- Reclamation proof: Docker's own shutdown log at 15:00:01 recorded `host-side disk file size 192.74 GB, VM-side disk usage 5.76 GB, threshold: 16 GB`; 9.8 seconds later it recorded `data disk vhdx compacted`. Windows VHDMP events 50 and 51 independently record the compact operation and its success. Afterward the non-sparse VHDX occupied 11.492 GiB and the first post-restart C: measurement showed 181.589 GiB free. The approximately 181.263 GiB VHDX reduction accounts for essentially the entire approximately 181.364 GiB host free-space recovery, with the Docker inventory still at zero containers, four images, zero volumes, and zero build cache.
- Attribution boundary: a retained 2026-07-08 12:47 artifact already measured the VHDX at about 200.0 GiB, proving that it was large before the later live-inventory cap. Compactions occurred later on July 8, followed by the heaviest recorded Docker write period, but no post-compaction VHDX-size snapshot was retained. It is therefore not possible to divide the 192.755 GiB high-water exactly between older allocation and the July 8–12 rebuild burst. It is proven that the small bounded state did not grow the file after July 13; it is also proven that recent maintenance-image churn materially contributed write pressure. Other C: writers may have consumed the remaining headroom, but no retained C:-free snapshot at the cleanup boundary can quantify them.
- Runtime consequence: the computer/Docker restart restored engine responsiveness after compaction. The earlier `host.docker.internal` DNS startup failure was a separate engine-start failure and is not attributed to disk pressure without evidence. No image, container, cache, workspace, VHDX block, or evidence artifact was deleted, pruned, moved, or manually compacted during the follow-up investigation.
- Approval boundary: the user explicitly requires approval before removal. No prune, move, compaction, or deletion may occur until an exact proposed action and preservation boundary are approved.
- Required action: measure VHDX host allocation, VM-side used space, Docker object inventory, and C: free space at bounded run checkpoints; retain Docker compaction events; and supervise the next pipeline sections at small scale. Any future removal or deliberate compaction remains separately approval-gated. The operational control should detect the live-use/host-allocation divergence without blocking useful throughput merely because cumulative I/O is high.
- Closure proof: two consecutive supervised pipeline cycles retain bounded live Docker objects, do not approach unsafe C: headroom, and either keep host allocation bounded or demonstrably reclaim internally free blocks through the normal Docker compaction lifecycle. This remains an operational qualification item, not a reason to halt unrelated analysis.

### BDS-169 - Stage 6 could accept an outcome-role ordering that ticket export rejects

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: delivery-blocking, contract parity
- Objective impact: a substantively valid, decision-complete plan passed Stage 6 but could not become a ticket because the singular original-scenario experiment was not first in the plural experiment list. Without an export-parity check, valid planning work reaches a deterministic dead end after the model has finished.
- Exact evidence: the first offline-first plan failed dry ticket assembly with `outcome_role_research_experiment_ids_invalid`; the plan used the README baseline as `research_experiment_id` while listing the direct baseline first in `research_experiment_ids`.
- Correction: Stage 6 validation now invokes the canonical outcome-role export normalizer and reports `change_plan_outcome_roles_export_contract_invalid` before accepting a plan. The same planner session received exact delta feedback and produced plan revision `planrev:sha256:448331253755408e0aa21d9018f24654fcd9350a039fd654a2a2421aafabd7a1`, with the singular direct baseline first and the README baseline retained second.
- Verification: the full planning-depth contract suite and Ruff pass; the corrected plan exported ready ticket `65683d49a62c3b59`.

### BDS-170 - The single-case materializer ignored Stage-5 option revisions

- Status: `closed_throwaway_harness_parity`
- Priority: supervision-efficiency, evidence fidelity
- Objective impact: the narrow validation path fed Stage 6 the stale Stage-4 option even though Stage 5 had revised that option to cover all six exact command occurrences. That could make a good pipeline result appear shallow or make the supervisor repair work the normal pipeline already performs.
- Exact evidence: Stage 5 `input_meta.option_revisions` contained the role-healed six-location option, while `.tmp/component_validation/materialize_report_schema_ticket.py` embedded the unrevised Stage-4 object.
- Correction: the throw-away materializer now applies and lineage-binds the exact Stage-5 revision before ticket assembly, matching the normal staged pipeline. No production option semantics were changed.
- Verification: the resulting ticket contains all six production/documentation locations and the exact Stage-5 selected mechanism.

### BDS-171 - Runner-owned option lineage invalidated an otherwise valid Stage-5 receipt

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: ticket-blocking, signed-receipt correctness
- Objective impact: adding server-owned `case_id`, canonical problem, and member IDs after Stage 5 changed the selected-option hash and made a valid falsification receipt look tampered. This blocks good throughput without detecting authored manipulation.
- Exact evidence: dry export failed with `selection_falsification_server_derived_claims_changed` and `selection_falsification_server_receipt_changed`; recomputation included lineage fields added only after the author signed the option.
- Correction: selected-option receipt views exclude exactly the runner-owned lineage annotations while continuing to hash all authored mechanism and outcome content.
- Verification: targeted falsifier-receipt tests prove server lineage is ignored and authored option tampering still fails; Ruff passes; ticket export succeeded.

### BDS-172 - Post-commit provenance treated runner-owned evidence and cache paths as implementation changes

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: PR-blocking, implementation provenance
- Objective impact: a correct implementation commit with passing original-scenario verification was prevented from reaching push/PR because provenance used a broader dirty-worktree definition than the commit path.
- Exact evidence: implementation commit `edad1e4d740258c1df2aaa4dc7bbd51df8837d60` passed all three runner-bound commands, but handoff stopped with `implementation_provenance_workspace_dirty_after_commit`. The only untracked content was 13 runner evidence files under `.usertest_run_dir` and 284 cache files under `pip/cache`; `commit_all` already excluded both.
- Correction: implementation provenance now uses the same runner-owned Git pathspec exclusions as commit finalization for both newly committed and observed-head modes.
- Verification: eight provenance tests pass, including rejection of an unrelated untracked file after runner-owned paths are ignored; Ruff passes. The retained commit then bound successfully without a model rerun.

### BDS-173 - A post-commit handoff exception can abort before writing durable resume state

- Status: `open_nonblocking_after_current_recovery`
- Priority: recovery-efficiency, lifecycle continuity
- Objective impact: when a deterministic contract check fails after commit, `run.py` raises before `handoff_summary.json` and `ticket_resume_state.json` are written. The exact implementation/session/workspace remain available, but the public resume command cannot continue at push/PR and would otherwise encourage a wasteful model rerun or manual GitHub work.
- Exact evidence: the offline-first implementation stopped at `commands/run.py` provenance binding after commit and verification. The run retained `git_ref.json`, verification, exact session, and workspace but had no resume state; existing resume paths accepted only failed verification or an already-created PR.
- Current containment: a one-time throw-away continuation invoked the pipeline's own provenance, push, CI, PR, ticket-move, ledger, and resume-state functions without rerunning the implementation model. Future occurrences of the exact dirty-path defect are prevented by BDS-172.
- Required action: persist a typed post-commit handoff-pending resume state before raising, and provide a no-model continuation that starts at the earliest incomplete handoff step.
- Closure proof: an induced post-commit failure writes a resume state and the public CLI completes push/PR without another author turn.

### BDS-174 - PR creation passed a multi-megabyte body through the Windows command line

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: PR-blocking, transport correctness
- Objective impact: the system completed implementation, verification, push, and CI but could not start `gh` because the generated ticket was passed as one command-line argument and exceeded the Windows process limit.
- Exact evidence: the first PR continuation failed with WinError 206 inside `subprocess`; `_run_gh` misleadingly converted that `FileNotFoundError` subclass to `gh not found on PATH`.
- Correction: production PR creation writes `pr_body.md` and invokes `gh pr create --body-file`.
- Verification: focused successful-PR and missing-`gh` tests pass and assert body-file transport; Ruff passes. The retained run advanced to GitHub's own body validation, proving the OS transport limit was removed.

### BDS-175 - Full generated tickets exceed GitHub's PR body contract

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: PR-blocking, review usability
- Objective impact: even body-file transport cannot publish a 2.9 MB generated ticket because GitHub caps PR bodies at 65,536 characters. Embedding the full audit corpus also obscures the actual causal change and verification plan from reviewers.
- Exact evidence: GitHub rejected PR creation with `Body is too long (maximum is 65536 characters)`; the source ticket contained 2,906,166 characters, dominated by retained research/audit history.
- Correction: the full ticket remains in the local `pr_manifest.md` with a SHA-256 receipt, while the PR receives a bounded causal projection containing the problem, success criteria, exact change targets, original-scenario proof, compatibility/failure modes, outcome verification, evidence IDs, and full-ticket hash.
- Verification: the real PR body is 45,859 bytes, the full manifest remains 2.9 MB, projection tests prove required causal sections and full-source retention, and pipeline-produced PR #219 was created successfully.

### BDS-176 - Initial review missed durable live evidence already present in raw events

- Status: `current_case_closed_systemic_transport_open`
- Priority: review-throughput, evidence discoverability
- Objective impact: the reviewer correctly assessed the mechanism but requested changes because it saw the plan's stale `planned_unverified` role marker and did not find the implementation run's completed live observation. Good evidence existed, but its location was not transported as a first-class outcome receipt.
- Exact evidence: initial review `20260720T161555Z/codex/0` reported live Claude unverified. Implementation raw event `item_19` records the exact live command, exit 0, and structured predicates: README read, one relative invocation, child exit 0, report present, zero absolute retry, no mangled path, and no repository change.
- Current correction: exact evidence plus the one genuinely missing `pwsh` check was returned to the same reviewer session `019f8050-bd38-7ff1-9345-12e546d8f05d`. It inspected the retained event, executed the exact PowerShell 7.6.3 command, and revised its own decision to approved with causal path closed and zero findings.
- Required action: promote completed implementation outcome commands into durable handoff receipts or explicit review evidence pointers so the initial reviewer does not rediscover them by broad artifact search.
- Closure proof: a later independent case's initial review consumes implementation live-role evidence without supervisor guidance or a redundant execution.

### BDS-177 - External implementation run roots are not propagated to terminal outcome trust

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: post-merge lifecycle, provenance continuity
- Objective impact: all required post-merge roles passed and the PR merged, but the terminal outcome was reported blocked because review/outcome processes trusted only the controller's default `runs` directory and rejected the explicitly selected `U:` implementation run root.
- Exact evidence: the first merge run produced merge commit `bc70b15bf1f6709a589d308f0739c2b865a65e77` and passed original, live, and mitigation roles, then failed terminal provenance with four `outside_trusted_roots` errors for `U:\usertest_implement_depth\runs\...` receipts.
- Current correction: the existing explicit `USERTEST_IMPLEMENT_OUTCOME_TRUSTED_RUNS_ROOT=U:\usertest_implement_depth\runs` boundary was supplied and `review merge` resumed the already-merged PR. It ran zero roles, revalidated the retained receipts, and completed the expected `mitigated` outcome in 27.3 seconds.
- Correction: qualification preparation now seals each explicit additional semantic evidence root and propagates those sealed roots into outcome synchronization. Trust still comes only from explicit configuration plus the immutable qualification manifest; mutable ticket text cannot add roots.
- Verification: the focused outcome-aggregation suite passes. Fresh preparation `bc70b15b_20260720T175050Z` sealed three additional roots, and its prepared case registry retained `case:adc507afeea214f0d1e4` as provenance-verified `mitigated` using the cross-volume implementation outcome without a supervisor-supplied outcome-trust variable.
- Closure proof: the fresh qualification preparation preserved the exact completed-case outcome and plan revision instead of downgrading or reopening it.

### BDS-178 - Qualification rejected an explicit evidence root whose sole target slug differed from the primary target

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: regeneration-blocking, provenance correctness
- Objective impact: a legitimate controller implementation run could not enter the sealed corpus because its canonical target slug was `usertest_backlog_depth` while the primary observation corpus target was `usertest`. Omitting the root would silently drop outcome evidence; pretending it belonged to the primary target would falsify lineage.
- Exact evidence: qualification preparation rejected `U:\usertest_implement_depth\runs` after it was explicitly added to cover the completed controller implementation outcome. The root contained one canonical target and five outcome paths, all under `usertest_backlog_depth`.
- Correction: explicit additional evidence roots prefer the primary target when present; otherwise they may bind to a different target only when that target is the sole canonical target in the root. The actual target slug is signed into the manifest. Ambiguous multi-target roots still fail.
- Verification: focused transaction tests cover cross-target acceptance, inventory binding, and ambiguous-root rejection. Fresh preparation sealed the root as target `usertest_backlog_depth`, one run, five outcome paths.

### BDS-179 - Qualification preparation required a manually pre-copied atom-action ledger

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: regeneration-blocking, transaction usability
- Objective impact: a fresh sealed run stopped after several minutes because the transaction expected a copied atom-action ledger that the preparation command itself did not create. Manual copying would be an unnecessary intervention and could break source/seed identity.
- Exact evidence: the first corrected `bc70b15b` preparation stopped with `qualification_input_requires_copied_atom_actions` after source inventory work completed.
- Correction: `qualification-prepare` now snapshots the live ledger into its work directory as write-once `backlog_atom_actions.seed.yaml`, verifies source stability, and rejects conflicting existing bytes.
- Verification: focused tests prove the copy, write-once conflict, and source-change rejection. Fresh preparation completed with 2,854 sealed eligible atoms, zero model calls, and zero ticket mutations.

### BDS-180 - Initial Stage-1 disagreement remained unresolved instead of using the retained same-author repair path

- Status: `contained_baseline_supervision_required`
- Priority: correct-disposition throughput, self-healing
- Objective impact: when the primary miner promoted a shallow claim and the independent reviewer rejected it, conservative reconciliation preserved safety but left three atoms `unresolved`. Without a focused continuation, the next cycle would repeat the same evidence instead of reaching an evidence-bounded disposition.
- Exact evidence: Sol/high slice `stage12_slice_mixed_sol_high_20260720T181500Z` processed eight eligible atoms. The primary proposed `problem:maintenance-cleanup-zero-deletions` from three zero-deletion observations while explicitly lacking deletion eligibility and physical-retention evidence. The independent reviewer produced zero problems and deferred those same atoms. Reconciliation emitted zero problems but left exactly those three atoms unresolved; the other five decisions agreed.
- Current containment: machine-produced reviewer decisions were returned to the exact primary session, then the revised result was automatically returned to the exact reviewer session. Both sessions converged on three `deferred` decisions, zero problems, and zero remaining unresolved targets in 118.2 seconds. No work was restarted or discarded.
- Required action: after baseline end-to-end throughput is established, make primary/reviewer disagreement automatically invoke this retained same-author correction path within the owning Stage 1 boundary. Bound attempts and preserve a repairable frontier if disagreement remains; do not turn disagreement into a global hard failure.
- Closure proof: a later unsupervised Stage 1 disagreement converges through the same two retained sessions, or exits with a durable repairable frontier, without supervisor-authored conclusions.

### BDS-181 - The first disagreement-correction wrapper read the wrong persisted reviewer field

- Status: `closed_throwaway_wrapper`

- Objective impact: the first correction launch stopped before a model call and added a needless diagnostic iteration.
- Exact evidence: the wrapper read `review_atom_decisions`; the persisted composite contract uses `coverage_depth_review_atom_decisions`. The source frontier remained byte-identical and the failed output was retained.

### BDS-182 - Narrow supervisor launches were not reproducible from a clean shell

- Status: `contained_supervisor_process`
- Priority: supervisor-efficiency, non-product
- Objective impact: three premodel diagnostics consumed avoidable iterations even though no pipeline or author work had failed.
- Exact evidence: the host PowerShell did not support `ConvertFrom-Json -Depth`; the first Stage-4 preflight omitted the monorepo package `src` paths and failed importing `backlog_core`; the second manually typed Stage-4 command used `U:\usert_backlog_qualification` instead of `U:\usertest_backlog_qualification`. A later focused pytest launch repeated the missing-package-path mistake and failed during collection before any test ran. On 2026-07-21, the first bounded Stage-2 launch again omitted the source roots and imported the installed `backlog_core`, stopping before argument parsing with `cannot import name 'research_actionability_assessment'`. The failed root was retained and no model call occurred.
- Current containment: JSON inspection uses Python on this host; narrow Python launches construct `PYTHONPATH` from every `packages/*/src` plus the owning app; stage paths are derived from one cycle-root variable instead of repeated literals. Every failed root was retained and no model call, ticket mutation, Docker action, or source-artifact change occurred.
- Required action: keep these environment and path derivations in reusable throw-away launchers during baseline qualification. Promote them only if the public component-run interface is intentionally productized.
- Closure proof: the subsequent Stage-4 preflight, live Stage 4, focused 29-test run, and exact retained-disposition replay all completed from the derived launch environment.

### BDS-183 - Stage-3 preflight did not exercise cross-volume Git ownership validation

- Status: `open_nonblocking`
- Priority: launch-efficiency, premodel correctness
- Objective impact: a live research launch failed before the model because Git rejected the clean `U:` worktree as dubious ownership, although the immediately preceding preflight had passed.
- Exact evidence: `stage3_case_131290_sol_high_20260720T183200Z` stopped before dispatch in repository-ref resolution. Preflight `stage3_case_131290_preflight_20260720T183100Z` had not executed that same resolution boundary. The same gap recurred after the host reboot: `stage3_case_dc5bf_sol_high_20260720T230232Z` passed preflight, then its first live launch stopped in 3.5 seconds with Git's `dubious ownership` rejection and zero model calls.
- Current containment: the exact clean worktree `U:/usertest_backlog_qualification/execution_roots/bc70b15b` is now registered in the host Git safe-directory list and resolves to `bc70b15bf1f6709a589d308f0739c2b865a65e77`. The successful research receipt binds that exact revision. This contains the current worktree but does not close the missing preflight-parity defect.
- Required action: Stage-3 preflight should exercise the exact repository-ref/Git ownership path used by live execution and report a premodel launch error there.
- Closure proof: an induced cross-volume qualification preflight either succeeds under an explicit scoped trust receipt or fails before the live launch with the same typed reason.

### BDS-184 - Stage 2 can rank a carried case whose source atom is absent from the sealed corpus

- Status: `open_nonblocking`
- Priority: prioritization quality, throughput
- Objective impact: a case with no current source observation can consume ranking attention or be selected ahead of evidence-backed work, even though research cannot authenticate its current frontier from the sealed atom set.
- Exact evidence: the three-case Stage-2 slice ranked the storage-exhaustion case P2 and selected it for `research_new`, while its deterministic score recorded zero current observations and its source atom was absent from the 2,854-atom bundle. The evidenced model-message aggregate case was correctly ranked P0 and selected first, so this did not block the current case.
- Required action: make missing current source evidence an explicit prioritization signal and prevent it from outranking evidence-backed cases unless the route is specifically an evidence-recovery mission. Do not silently delete the carried case.
- Closure proof: a narrow mixed slice keeps the carried identity traceable but does not select it as ordinary problem research until its source frontier is present or an explicit recovery route is chosen.

### BDS-185 - Aggregate-backed research could never hydrate for a later cycle

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: lifecycle correctness, repeated-work prevention
- Objective impact: correct Stage-3 research over one canonical aggregate atom plus supporting occurrences was treated as stale on the next cycle, forcing the same expensive research to repeat.
- Exact evidence: completed case `case:131290268e2889891e0c` had one canonical `source_evidence_atom_id`, while its authenticated assignment contained that atom plus 14 occurrence atoms. Retained hydration compared the canonical case frontier to all 15 assigned IDs and returned `retained_research_source_evidence_frontier_mismatch`.
- Correction: hydration compares the case frontier and current hashes to the assignment's explicit `case_evidence_atom_ids`; it still validates every assigned receipt, and legacy assignments without the role field retain the previous all-IDs behavior. The aggregate hash remains the currentness authority and binds its occurrence set.
- Verification: a new aggregate-plus-occurrence regression and the full focused hydration/downstream suite pass. The exact retained dossier now hydrates with zero errors.

### BDS-186 - Retained hydration rejected runner-enriched Stage-3 dossiers

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: lifecycle correctness, contract consistency
- Objective impact: a persisted dossier accepted by Stage 3 and Stage 4 could not be reused because runner-owned lineage fields were passed into the strict authored-dossier schema as unknown fields.
- Exact evidence: after BDS-185 was corrected, the exact case advanced to readiness validation and failed with `research_dossier_unknown_fields` for `canonical_problem_id` and `case_member_problem_ids`, routing the case back to `research_update`.
- Correction: retained hydration keeps the full enriched dossier hash and identity checks, then applies the same authored `research_contract_view` used by Stages 4-6 for readiness and persisted-evidence validation.
- Verification: a new lineage-enriched regression and the focused hydration/downstream suite pass. Exact replay now authenticates `runner_retained_no_change_v1`, routes the case to `await_evidence`, selects neither research nor downstream work, and assembles zero tickets.

### BDS-187 - Occurrence-only research was mistaken for an empty retained evidence frontier

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: lifecycle correctness, repeated-work prevention
- Objective impact: ordinary cases with direct source observations could complete sound research and a zero-option disposition, then be forced back into Stage 3 on the next cycle because their intentionally empty aggregate-evidence list was interpreted as an empty case frontier. That wastes a correct dossier and defeats stable throughput.
- Exact evidence: completed Claude case `case:01fc33f2c4cf70160dfb` has three current source atoms. Its authenticated assignment partitions those same three atoms into `occurrence_evidence_atom_ids` and correctly leaves `case_evidence_atom_ids` empty. The first exact replay returned `retained_research_source_evidence_frontier_mismatch` and routed `research_update` despite unchanged evidence, an evidence-sufficient dossier, and an authenticated `already_addressed` disposition.
- Correction: retained hydration now uses signed case-level IDs when an operational aggregate exists, otherwise the signed occurrence IDs are the durable case frontier. Legacy dossiers without an available signed partition still use the complete authenticated assignment. This applies the existing general evidence-role contract; it does not special-case Claude or weaken currentness checks.
- Verification: a new two-occurrence regression passes alongside the aggregate-with-supporting-occurrences regression; 50 adjacent hydration/downstream/historical lifecycle tests pass; the full `apps/usertest_backlog` suite passes with two expected skips in 300.6 seconds. Exact replay of the retained Claude dossier hydrates all three current atoms with zero errors and routes `await_evidence`, with Stage 3 deselected, downstream disabled, and no model, ticket, export, implementation, Docker, or removal action.

### BDS-188 - The bounded group materializer initially projected only the research-unit case

- Status: `closed_throwaway_wrapper`
- Priority: supervisor-efficiency, provisional-group fidelity
- Objective impact: the first bounded Windows launch copied only the selected research-unit record and preserved stale one-member problem metadata, so live Stage 3 stopped before a model call even though the registry already described a two-case provisional group.

The preceding component preflight had passed. The later relation receipt helper also initially inferred the absorbed case from member ordering instead of the returned alias mapping; that receipt-only error was caught before it was used downstream.
- Correction: the throw-away materializer invokes the production provisional-research-unit membership synchronizer, and the relation helper derives absorbed IDs from the production `case_aliases` mapping.
- Verification: the corrected Stage-2 packet retained both cases, dispatched one research unit, assigned all 25 source atoms, and the corrected relation receipt identifies `case:7df34a9a76b8de621aed` as the sole absorbed alias of `case:fe517dafc2a12d5779ab`.

### BDS-189 - Stage-3 preflight did not validate the exact provisional-group assignment

- Status: `open_nonblocking`
- Priority: launch-efficiency, premodel correctness
- Objective impact: a component preflight can report success while the immediately following live Stage-3 invocation rejects the provisional member/problem mapping before dispatch. This wastes a launch iteration but does not corrupt evidence or generate bad work.
- Exact evidence: `stage3_group_windows_preflight_20260720T210700Z` completed with zero calls, while the earlier structurally equivalent one-member packet passed preflight and then failed live with `provisional_same_cause_record_problem_members_mismatch`.

### BDS-190 - Post-research relation identity ignored authenticated proof-adapter causality

- Status: `closed`
- Priority: canonical-case correctness, downstream deduplication
- Objective impact: Stage 3 could produce a runner-verified primary mechanism covering every provisional member atom, yet post-research collapse still left the same-cause group unresolved because causal identity recognized controls, falsification interventions, closures, and probe control points but not verified proof-adapter interventions and causal edges.
- Exact evidence: the corrected Windows dossier was evidence-sufficient with one verified mechanism receipt covering 25/25 assigned source atoms, a content-bound intervention target, and a verifier-produced causal edge. `verify_persisted_research_evidence` and `assess_research_readiness` both returned true with zero errors, while `_verified_causal_signature` returned `None` and the production collapse emitted no group.
- Correction: the causal projection now accepts a proof-adapter route only when the verified provenance contains both a selected causal-root edge and an intervention target. It normalizes stable target, caller, callee, path, symbol, and edge-kind facts while excluding case-local IDs and hashes. An incomplete adapter with a target but no verified edge still cannot collapse cases.
- Verification: two focused regressions cover accepted and incomplete adapters; all 21 post-research relation tests pass. Exact production replay aliases `case:7df34a9a76b8de621aed` to `case:fe517dafc2a12d5779ab`, reduces two priority records to one downstream unit, and resolves the canonical identity. The full backlog application suite passed 838 tests with two expected skips in 394.8 seconds.

### BDS-191 - External relation feedback was marked corrected before its predicate was reevaluated

- Status: `open_nonblocking_baseline_supervised`
- Priority: self-healing accuracy, correction efficiency
- Objective impact: the retained same-author correction loop considered an external relation finding resolved when the authored schema and native evidence verifier returned zero fatal errors, even though the downstream relation predicate still reported zero verified origin atoms. This produced two avoidable supervisor feedback turns and misleading intermediate `corrected` receipts.
- Exact evidence: the first retained correction added a controlled proof adapter over all 25 atoms, but its positive repository quote was unattested; the second fixed the quote but omitted the experiment-level positive-outcome contract. Both continuation receipts reported `corrected` and zero native validation errors, while rerunning the downstream predicate still returned 0/25. The third same-session correction added the exact contract and produced 25/25 verified mechanism coverage.
- Current containment: every external correction is checked by rerunning the function that produced the finding; native zero-error status alone is not treated as proof that an external predicate changed. All three corrections retained session `019f8159-b871-7990-acbf-4d3d5a4badcd`; none restarted or discarded the dossier.
- Required action: external feedback must carry a machine-evaluable recheck contract, and `corrected` should be emitted only when that predicate passes or the author supplies an authenticated alternative disposition. Changed error identities and reduced error counts remain progress and keep the same author frontier.
- Closure proof: a future post-stage finding automatically reexecutes its bound verifier after each same-author correction and continues from the retained frontier until the predicate passes, becomes genuinely uncorrectable, or reaches the configured nonprogress policy.

### BDS-192 - Bounded Stage-3 continuation lineage retained a stale research self-reference

- Status: `closed_throwaway_wrapper`
